## Execution order

Follow `0 → 0b → 1 → 2 → 3 → 3b → 4 → 5 → 6 → 7 → 8 → 9 → 10 → 11 → 12a → 12b`, then **15 core → 16 → 17 health → 13 production integration → 18 preview**. Keep full-only work pending until its implementation and gates pass. IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN owns exact scope and ordering; the table below is a tracking aid.

### 2026-10-15 — First CI runs (commits e62c2fe, 03c632d)

- GitHub Actions run 1 failed and exposed three real defects, each reproduced and fixed with a regression test: (1) Linux: `/proc/<pid>/environ` can read empty while a launcher shim execs the CLI (3/150 local runs under CPU load; 0/150 after re-sampling, empty reads now reported as unobservable); (2) Windows: tests read UTF-8 fixtures with the cp1252 default, fixed with explicit UTF-8 and LF-pinning `.gitattributes`; (3) macOS: temp paths resolve through `/private`, so native-memory/prior-session sentinels keyed by the unresolved path were never found; the probe now plants both (symlinked-tempdir test fails before, passes after).
- Run 3 (03c632d): all five jobs green — Linux Python 3.12, 3.13, 3.14; macOS and Windows on 3.13 (probe end-to-end suites skip on Windows; native Windows host probes remain to be run). Local: 146 passed on 3.12, 3.13 and 3.14.

### 2026-10-16 — Task 2 preview slice: installations, store, bootstrap

- Files: `src/recalith/core/{identity,paths}.py`, `src/recalith/storage/{db,repository}.py`, `src/recalith/storage/migrations/0001.sql`, `src/recalith/__main__.py` (interim: every command `capability_unavailable`), `bootstrap.py`, `requirements.lock`, `tests/integration/test_installations.py`, `tests/platform/test_paths.py`, `docs/decisions/0003-installation-identity-and-provisioning.md`; DATA_CONTRACTS §12 and IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN Task 1 gain the `installation_relocated` code and the operator `workspace rebind|discard-copy` forms; CI gains a requirements-lock sync check and an offline provisioning step.
- Behavior: `initialize_installation` creates the layout with private permissions, a self-contained `.recalith/.gitignore` (tracks only `project.toml`, `engine.lock`, `bin/`), POSIX/Windows launchers, and a fresh store built on a temp file and renamed into place, then writes `local.toml` last. It refuses an existing store, completes an interrupted init, and refuses known network filesystems on Linux. `resolve_installation` takes exactly the given root and classifies it as same, moved or copied by file identity. A clone keeps the project ID and gets a new installation; a move needs an operator rebind; a copy is refused and can only be discarded. The store uses WAL, `synchronous=FULL`, foreign keys, a 250 ms busy timeout and STRICT tables. Writes check identity, schema, policy and tombstone epochs and expected revisions. A newer schema is neither written nor read. The repository persists events exactly once (duplicate vs `source_conflicts`) and rejects foreign installations and workspaces.
- `.venv/bin/python -m pytest -q -rs` → exit 0, 172 passed, 3 skipped (opt-in provisioning; Linux case-insensitive variant; Windows junction). Integration and platform suites also pass on Python 3.12 (SQLite 3.45.1) and 3.14 (SQLite 3.53.1).
- Real provisioning: built the wheel (`uv build`), downloaded the hash-locked wheelhouse, then ran `bootstrap.py install`. A bad digest exits 2; install exits 0 in about 4 s; a re-run reports `already_provisioned`; the launcher exits 4 with `capability_unavailable` before provisioning and runs the pinned runtime after. The opt-in test passed with `RECALITH_TEST_WHEEL`/`RECALITH_TEST_WHEELHOUSE` set.
- CI run 5 (9c53ff1): all five jobs green. Linux 3.12/3.13/3.14 passed 172 with 3 skips; the 3.13 job also passed the offline provisioning step and the requirements-lock sync check. macOS 3.13 passed 172 with 3 skips, and the APFS case-insensitive path test ran. Windows 3.13 passed 135 with 40 skips; the NTFS case-insensitive, junction-escape, symlink-swap and `.cmd` launcher tests all ran, and the skips are the POSIX-only fake-host probe suites, POSIX permission bits and the opt-in provisioning test.
- Acceptance: A01, A02 (schema), A03, A04 (store paths), A33 and A53 (launcher) are `in_progress` with tests listed; none is passed, since later owning tasks remain. Coverage gaps: no casefold filesystem could be mounted in this container (`mount -t tmpfs -o casefold` fails), so case-insensitive coverage relies on the macOS/Windows CI runners; junction coverage is Windows-only.

### 2026-10-16 — Task 3: durable event capture and source cursors

- Files: `src/recalith/storage/{spool,failpoints}.py` (stdlib only, for reuse by the Task 3b fast path), `src/recalith/capture/{redact,normalize,parsers,sources,provenance,pipeline}.py`, and additions to `storage/repository.py` (`event_from_row`, `idempotency_key`, `enqueue_job`). ID prefixes `seg`/`obs` are registered. Tests: `tests/unit/test_{redaction,normalize}.py`, `tests/integration/test_{capture_recovery,runtime_provenance}.py`, and the test-only source and crash driver `tests/integration/capture_scenarios.py`. Workflow: `.github/workflows/nightly.yml` (20 reps per failpoint). Decision record `docs/decisions/0004-capture-spool-and-normalization.md`. DATA_CONTRACTS §2.1/§2.2/§11 and IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN Task 3 updated to match.
- Behavior: `capture(ctx, events)` re-checks ownership and derived identity, then scrubs again. A secret in an identity field refuses that event. Capture applies the environment allowlist, packs segments of at most 256 KiB, and publishes each one (temp write, fsync (`F_FULLFSYNC` on macOS), atomic rename, directory fsync on POSIX). Windows gets one transient-rename retry, waiting at most 20 ms within the soft deadline. Capture acknowledges only after that, then advances the cursor file. Any failure returns `not_captured` with a content-free reason and records a gap. `ingest_ready(ctx, limit=100)` handles each segment in one transaction: events (duplicate vs `source_conflicts`), sessions, environment observations, cursor proposals and one idempotent `ingest` job. It then unlinks the segment. Invalid or foreign segments are quarantined. Normalization replaces invalid Unicode and scrubs before anything else. It keeps 8 KiB head and 8 KiB tail on code-point boundaries with exact original-offset chunks, and puts a marker in the display string. Structured fields win over text within the 64 KiB cap, and oversized messages are partitioned. Typed assertions, the ≤1 KiB error-signature line and runtime versions come only from certified parsers (pytest, unittest, exact `python`/`node --version`). Raw assertions and the `expected_red` hint are dropped with coverage warnings. Registered sources are exact files: a symlink is refused, only complete lines are consumed, and truncation, rewrite or replacement starts a new generation with an explicit gap. Runtime provenance is either declared (`operator_config`, current for its config revision) or observed (`certified_tool`, bound to a registered executable fingerprint). A registered fingerprint change marks older observations stale and advances `guidance_epoch`. Unbound, stale and conflicting values resolve as unknown. Recalith's interpreter is never consulted.
- Commands (Linux x86_64, SQLite 3.45.1):
  - `.venv/bin/python -m pytest -q -rs` (Python 3.13.16) → exit 0, 286 passed, 4 skipped. The skips are the opt-in provisioning test, Linux case-insensitive, the Windows junction, and real `EACCES` as root.
  - `UV_PROJECT_ENVIRONMENT=<scratch> uv run --locked --python 3.12 python -m pytest -q -rs` → exit 0, 286 passed, 4 skipped. The same on 3.14 → exit 0, 286 passed, 4 skipped.
  - `RECALITH_CRASH_REPS=20 .venv/bin/python -m pytest -q -rs tests/integration/test_capture_recovery.py -k crash_failpoints` → exit 0, 11 passed in 135 s. That is 220 kill/restart runs: each of 11 failpoints across spool temp write, fsync, rename, after-rename, after-publish, cursor write, after-cursor-write, ingest begin, before commit, after commit and after unlink. Each run is seeded with partial lines, a duplicate producer and a changed-content conflict. Each one kills a real child process (`os._exit`), restarts, and compares the reopened store with a reference run: same events and digests, conflicts, one job reference per event, cursor, sessions and observations, and an empty spool. A deliberate mutation (cursor before publication) failed 10 of these tests, which confirms the matrix detects reordering.
  - The four `EACCES`/permission tests were run as `nobody` via `runuser` → 4 passed; the real-permission variant skips as root. `uv run --locked python tools/generate_schemas.py --check` → exit 0. `uvx ruff@0.14.0 check --select F,E9,B` on the new files → clean.
- CI run 9 (commit 4941d4d): all five jobs green. - Host facts that changed the design (decision 0005): no payload carries the host version. `prompt_id` repeats for queued prompts. Background-subagent completion arrives as a host-generated `<task-notification>` prompt, which gets no user authority. `PostToolUse` never certifies exit 0, because `grep` exit 1 arrives as success. Bash stdout is head-capped at 30,000 characters (`persistedOutputSize`), and failing output is middle-truncated to about 10,000 characters with a marker, so the tail of failing output above about 30,000 characters is lost before any hook sees it. Recalith flags this as truncated/incomplete. `PermissionRequest` has no `tool_use_id` or outcome and becomes an explicit gap, and a denied call has no post-tool hook. The mutation corpus also found a strict-contract defect: Pydantic literals accepted `true`/`1.0` for `1` and `0` for `false`, so `expected_red: 0` passed. That is fixed with type-exact literals and pinned by fixtures.

- Lifecycle observed per OS (CI run 21 plus local):
  - **Linux**: new session; it survives a `killpg` of the simulated host's group, and the control process in that group dies, so the kill was effective; ingestion continues afterwards. Inherited pipes are closed (the host sees EOF). The kernel `/proc/<pid>/environ` matches the reported key names.
  - **macOS**: the same tests pass. macOS itself adds `__CF_USER_TEXT_ENCODING` to every process; it is not inherited through the allowlist.
  - **Windows**: `DETACHED_PROCESS | CREATE_NEW_PROCESS_GROUP | CREATE_BREAKAWAY_FROM_JOB` with a null-device handle list. The worker survived termination of a simulated host's kill-on-close job that permits breakaway, and ingested afterwards. CI run 20 caught a false claim: with a host job that forbids breakaway, the flag was still accepted (CPython's venv `python.exe` redirector runs the hook in a nested job, so breakaway leaves only that job), and the worker died with the host while its status said detached. The worker now verifies itself with `IsProcessInJob` and claims `detached` only outside every job; otherwise it reports `degraded` (`job_membership_unverified`). On CI it reports degraded, and the honesty test passes.
- Latency (`python tools/measure_worker_latency.py`, exit 0; `docs/measurements/worker-typed-state-latency.json`). Measured on the 4-vCPU Linux container, Python 3.13.16, with the real hook-started worker and real 2.1.301 prompt payloads arriving at uniformly random phases of the idle 5 s cycle (n=100). Typed state (event committed and its ingest job completed), timed from hook acknowledgment: p50 2.76 / p95 4.74 / p99 4.90 / max 4.97 s, which meets the preview p95 ≤ 5 s target. From hook spawn: p95 4.79 s. Hook wall time (n=30): starting the worker p50 62.4 / p95 86.7 ms; with a worker already running p50 58.0 / p95 72.0 ms. Idle worker over 600 s: 0.14 % of one core and 38.3 MiB RSS (targets < 1 % and ≤ 150 MiB). The CI test uses 8 evenly spaced phases with p95 ≤ 5 s and passed on all five jobs. The new module adds about 0.6 ms to the hook's imports.

### 2026-10-16 — Task 5 preview slice: mutation application and optimistic concurrency

- Files: `src/recalith/controller/{apply,admission}.py`, `src/recalith/core/policy.py`, `src/recalith/storage/records.py` (revision/head/link writers), `src/recalith/storage/migrations/0002.sql` (schema 2: proposal digest and read set, operation local ref and digest, claim content key). Contracts: `ModelResponse.malformed` (`MalformedOperation`), `parse_model_body_partitioned`, `UndoPreview`/`UndoChange`/`UndoConflict` (schemas regenerated, 45 snapshots). Changed: `controller/worker.py` (`ModelRuntime` has no pluggable applier; the lane persists and applies through `apply`), `controller/budgets.py` (`decode_body` is per-operation), `controller/jobs.py` (`supersede`, inherited review priority on enqueue), `fast/failpoints.py` (`proposal.before_commit`, `apply.after_group` in `WORKER`; `service.before_commit` in the new `SERVICE` family), `storage/db.py` (`SCHEMA_VERSION = 2`). Tests: `tests/integration/test_operations.py`, `tests/integration/test_integrity.py` (full-only tests skipped as unavailable), builders `tests/integration/operation_scenarios.py`; `test_worker.py` and `worker_scenarios.py` now drive the real application path. Docs: decision 0007, DATA_CONTRACTS §7/§7.1/§8/§10/§11, IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN Task 5 files.
- Behavior: the proposal and the `ReadSet` it was prepared under are persisted under the lease fence before application. `apply_proposal` runs inside `jobs.fenced`. In one transaction it checks the lease, locates the persisted proposal, rechecks the read set (policy revision, tombstone epoch, consulted heads, `file:` fingerprints) and every target revision, then applies the dependency-graph components as atomic groups, each in a savepoint and in topological order. It writes deterministic operation IDs (`job` + proposal digest + local ref) and one `@apply` application record holding the `ApplyResult`, advances `store_revision` (and `guidance_epoch` only when a memory record changed), and completes the job. A committed application returns its original result before any lease check. A conflict applies nothing and queues one coalesced re-evaluation through `jobs.enqueue` (at most 3 per chain, then `reevaluation_limit`); a service writer (`apply_operations`) receives `revision_conflict`. Admission is structural: the actor may apply the kind; target and payload agree; records exist in this installation; evidence is available, keeps its root identity, and quoted spans hash to their digests; scope is registered; status transitions are in the table; dispute and invalidation cite evidence; an observed claim names a mapping. A malformed or rejected operation fails only its group; a later rejection rolls back records its group already wrote. Proposed claims are weak candidates. Exact duplicates reuse the head and add only links with new root identity. `revise_claim` yields an unreviewed candidate revision. `assess_claim` changes nothing (`review_admission_unavailable`) and `resolve_task_binding` is rejected (`task_binding_unavailable`), both until Tasks 9 and 6. Undo: `preview_undo` diffs immutable before/after revisions, restores only fields still holding the undone value, reports later changes as conflicts, retires created records and removes added relations. `apply_undo` (operator actor, current preview digest) appends revisions as one undoable `undo` operation.
- Commands (Linux x86_64 container, SQLite 3.45.1; Claude Code CLI not installed in this container, so the two installed-host tests skip):
  - `.venv/bin/python -m pytest -q -rs` (Python 3.13.16) → exit 0, 414 passed, 13 skipped, 1 xfailed. The skips are the 8 from Task 4 (opt-in provisioning, Linux case-insensitive, Windows junction, two root-only `EACCES` variants, the Windows-only no-breakaway job test, two installed-CLI tests) plus the 5 full-only integrity tests. The xfail is still `test_five_candidates_use_one_review_call` (Task 9).
  - `UV_PROJECT_ENVIRONMENT=<scratch> uv run --locked --python 3.12 python -m pytest -q -rs` → exit 0, 414 passed, 13 skipped, 1 xfailed; the same on 3.14 → exit 0, 414 passed, 13 skipped, 1 xfailed. After the last test addition (path-escape fingerprints), `tests/integration/test_operations.py tests/integration/test_integrity.py` → 19 passed, 5 skipped on each of 3.12, 3.13 and 3.14.
  - As `nobody` via `runuser` with a traversable basetemp: the `-k permission` capture tests → 5 passed; `tests/platform/test_worker_lifecycle.py tests/integration/test_operations.py` → 29 passed, 2 skipped.
  - `RECALITH_CRASH_REPS=20 .venv/bin/python -m pytest -q tests/integration/test_worker.py -k crash` → exit 0, 7 passed in 178 s. That is 7 worker failpoints × 20 kill/restart runs. The crash project holds the real 2.1.301 lifecycle events (ingestion drained first, so kills land on model jobs), a seeded task and an operator-seeded claim. Four jobs propose an independent observed claim with a quoted span, an atomic replacement + supersession, a group citing a never-captured event, a malformed operation and its dependent, an episode and a task update. Each reopened store equals a reference run: job states, application records, operation rows, decisions, claim/task/episode heads, revision/relation/link counts, empty `revision_chain`, and the guidance epoch.
  - `tools/generate_schemas.py --check` and `tools/generate_hook_wire.py --check` → exit 0. `uvx ruff@0.14.0 check --select F,E9,B` on all new and touched files → clean, apart from the pre-existing `fast/redact.py` B905.

  - Caught by `test_operations.py`: no savepoint rollback (`test_replacement_and_retirement_commit_together`; it first slipped past, because every rejection fired before any write, so a late-failure case was added); no read-set check (3 tests); random operation IDs (3); no stale-target check (1); bookkeeping advancing guidance (2); no exact-duplicate reuse (1).
  - Caught by the crash matrix: lease bookkeeping advancing guidance (6 of 7 failpoints); ignoring the persisted proposal (`job.after_proposal`, `apply.after_group`, `job.before_complete_commit`); job completion moved to a second transaction (`job.before_complete_commit`). The last one first slipped past because kills landed on 33 pending ingest jobs; ingestion is now drained before the model jobs are enqueued.

- Subproject A gate (review focus rechecked against everything built so far; the cross-component suites ran in the full runs above):
  1. **Same source event twice across a crash: one support root, one mutation effect.** Holds for the kernel. Capture and fast-producer crash matrices (Tasks 3/3b) keep one event per source key. The real-operation worker matrix keeps one application per job. A duplicate proposal, or the same claim from another job citing the same root, adds no revision and no support link (`test_duplicate_proposal_has_one_effect`). Task 10 revision paths remain.
  2. **Quoted or repeated source gains no authority or corroboration.** Partially built. Task 5 refuses re-rooted links, gives delivery telemetry no roots, never refreshes support on a repeated root, and assigns no strength above `weak`. User-region and quotation handling and root classes are Tasks 8/9 (pending).
  3. **Correction/forget before the final delivery fence.** Mechanics only: invalidation and replacement commit with a guidance-epoch advance in one transaction, and stale revisions fail atomically. Delivery fences and forgetting are Tasks 12b/16; their tests remain explicitly pending, not passed.
  4. **Paths across platforms, worktrees and relocation.** Task 2 path and installation suites pass. - Behavior: the worker's ingest job reduces every committed, unreduced event in commit order inside its fenced transaction and applies everything through `controller.apply` (Task 5) as one atomic chain per application, with zero model calls. A new session opens a provisional task (`new_session`; `host_reset` after a host `/clear`); later events bind provisionally to the session's current task; a result joins its call by `tool_call_id` across splits and sessions. Typed facts cite their events: the first explicit request as objective (exact quote, `user_declared`), pending actions `awaiting_result`, known results with outcome, exit code, certified assertions and error signature (replaced per action signature, capped at 24), and test/build blockers that close only when the same action passes. Candidates (pivot prefix outside quoted/pasted starts, five disjoint path actions with the coding `@code` cluster and operator mappings, 30-minute idle resume, new session, host reset) are recorded pending and change no binding. At a new session's third path action, exactly one eligible task (active, or paused within 24 h; placeholders excluded) whose clusters contain all three actions gets a `single_active_task` link: the range, typed items and pending calls move; the target's objective and narrative do not; the provisional head becomes `linked` with `redirect_task_id`. A pair that spans sessions declines the link. Recorded `resolve_task_binding` proposals confirm a split (exact contiguous range of one session, new code-allocated task, both objectives kept), reject a candidate (events confirmed in place) or link/confirm a session; a range that would leave an arrived result behind its call is refused; a moved binding re-evaluates; links reverse by an explicit split or operator undo. Idle tasks pause after 24 h; activity reactivates; completion needs a `completion_basis` with evidence. At compaction, session end and pause the open range becomes a settled episode unless a test/build still fails or (except at session end) a call awaits its result; the checkpoint then waits for the fix; 64 steps flush an `open` episode whose successor carries its unresolved items as context. Successes keep exit code, assertions and an exact excerpt; carried context is never support; causes stay unassessed.

### 2026-10-17 — Task 8: source lineage and applicability (and the Subproject B gate)

- Files: new `src/recalith/core/{lineage,conditions}.py`, `src/recalith/storage/migrations/0004.sql` (schema 4: `root_event_members`, `root_tokens`, indexes on `root_members`/`root_aliases`), tests `tests/unit/test_lineage.py` (23), `tests/unit/test_conditions.py` (10), builders `tests/integration/lineage_scenarios.py`, and the kill/restart matrix `test_lineage_merge_crash_matches_reference` in `tests/integration/test_worker.py` (driver `worker_scenarios.py` counts the new family). Changed: `capture/pipeline.py` (root registration and merge-triggered revalidation inside the ingestion transaction), `controller/apply.py` (support attached only when it adds a root class; `last_supported_at`), `controller/admission.py` (record links may name only roots their provenance reaches), `contracts/common.py` (path conditions refuse glob syntax), `contracts/context.py` (`ContextQuery.branch_ref`), `fast/failpoints.py` (`failpoints.LINEAGE`: `roots.after_merge`), `storage/db.py` (`SCHEMA_VERSION = 4`). Schemas regenerated (46; `ContextQuery` changed); the hook wire tables are unchanged. Docs: decision 0010, DATA_CONTRACTS §1.1/§6.1/§9/§10/§11, SYSTEM_SPEC §7.1.1, IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN Task 8 files, coverage manifest.
- Behavior: lineage code derives a canonical observation descriptor from each stored, scrubbed envelope (the 2.1.301 adapter sets none; the hook path is unchanged). Eligible roots are authenticated user statements and certified tool *results* without `derived_from`. Never roots: memory telemetry, derived events (resolved through `derived_from`), assistant text, events without user/tool authority (uncertified versions, the host `<task-notification>` prompt, lifecycle), a call without its result, `echo`/`printf` output (agent text through a tool), and forwarded subagent output, which resolves to the subagent's own tool results (same host session, its `agent_id`). Ingestion registers each eligible root in the event's own transaction with exact tokens (origin; artifact snapshot for a complete, untruncated 2.1.301 `Read` of one path; complete-dependency experiment signature only for a certified domain, of which none exists; otherwise one unresolved experiment family per workspace and normalized action). Classes are the persisted transitive closure; the ID is `root_` + SHA-256 of the least canonical origin key, replaced IDs are kept as aliases, and a merge of counted classes advances guidance and queues a coalesced protected `revalidate` job for the live claims citing them. Counting traverses provenance with cycle detection and bounds; all of one user's declarations about a proposition are one class; unresolved descriptors add no class beside a resolved one; an unresolved source conflict excludes the root and flags revalidation; contradictions and mixed-outcome classes block corroboration. Applicability is tri-state Kleene AND with exclusions: exact paths or `/` prefixes (case/normalization near-misses unknown, nothing lowercased), registered environment keys, PEP 440 ranges through `packaging` (unparsable unknown unless `upper == value`), `action_kind` only for exact parses, explicit `branch_ref` predicates versus ignored `branch_refs` annotations, scope before similarity, the failure-lesson dependency gate (empty or unfingerprinted unknown, changed false on any branch), and caution only for `true` + current.
- Wired now versus consumer hooks: **wired** — registration at ingestion, merge revalidation enqueue, class-based support novelty for `attach_evidence` and duplicate `propose_claim` (`duplicate_evidence` / `no_independent_root`, nothing written), `last_supported_at`, the record-link root check. **Consumer hooks for later tasks** — `lineage.resolve` (`support_classes`, `corroboration_blocked`, `needs_revalidation`) for Task 9 strength and admission (claims stay weak candidates; `assess_claim` unchanged); the revalidation decision and the dependency index for Task 10; `conditions.applicability`/`in_scope`/`advice_for`/`dependency_applicability` for Task 11 retrieval and packets; `ContextQuery.branch_ref` supplied by Task 12b; forgetting the root tables in Task 16.

Changed: `controller/admission.py` (semantic gates, activation, strength), `controller/apply.py` (observed activation, derived failure bodies, strength upkeep, review queueing, the `assess_claim` handler, outcomes, scheduling inside the application), `controller/requests.py` (delegates review/extraction selection; rationale-free claim views keep an active claim's verdict), `controller/jobs.py` (`trigger_ref`), `controller/worker.py` (trigger reducer and sweep), `working/reducer.py` (extraction at settled episodes), `core/policy.py` (`assess_claim` mutating), `prompts/__init__.py` (file-backed versions), `fast/failpoints.py` (`failpoints.ADMISSION`), `storage/db.py` (`SCHEMA_VERSION = 5`). Earlier tests updated to the stricter semantics (observed facts are code renderings; seeded claims auto-schedule reviews; Task 5–8 matrices keep their own jobs). Docs: decision 0011, DATA_CONTRACTS §1.1/§5/§6.2/§8/§10/§11, SYSTEM_SPEC §7.1/§7.1.2/§7.2, IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN Task 9 files, coverage manifest.
- Behavior: see decision 0011. In short: an `observed` claim is a `fact` whose proposition is exactly the code rendering of its validated `tool.exit_code`/`tool.reported_result` mappings (admitted active without a model; `config.exact_value` refused, no parser); `user_declared` support spans must be direct, complete user statements outside fenced, quoted, attached, email, diff and pasted regions (span intersection, no first-person heuristic); failure-lesson bodies are code-derived; scope never exceeds the evidence's workspaces; every other claim is a weak candidate until a supported review; strength comes only from lineage classes; active guidance is superseded only by active guidance. Applications that write candidates schedule their review in the same transaction, in stable partitions of at most eight (output and exact 96 KiB input permitting), deferring a candidate too large to review; batches are persisted before launch; per-candidate assessments are admitted against the job's batch, the snapshot digest and the candidate's own evidence (missing/duplicate/foreign/stale never activate); revise records objections only; `review_capacity` keeps a candidate and schedules nothing; outcomes are recorded per candidate. Extraction runs at settled episodes and due triggers with bounded selection, folded boundary candidates and a deterministic no-op. The debounce is deterministic in event time (six relevant events / ten minutes, family match across changed dependencies, null family never matches, late successes, sweep).
- Wired now versus consumer hooks: **wired** — everything above, `review_candidates`, and the `provider_unavailable` candidate-only degradation. **Consumer hooks for later tasks** — Task 10 decides queued `revalidate` jobs, FailureRecheck, consolidation and narrowing/dispute/invalidation of active guidance; Task 11 serves active guidance and candidates only as labeled uncertainty; Task 12a supplies the guarded `declare`/`correct` transports and their operator events (same admission and review path); Task 12b fences delivery; Tasks 15/16 make the new tables participate in retention and forgetting. Semantic review of task completion statements is not built (batches hold claims; completion still needs cited evidence).

The scenario reduces the real lifecycle and a declaration prompt, spools a failure with six relevant results (due by count) and a fixed failure (resolved), and runs one extraction whose application admits an observed fact and schedules one batch of five; the review admits two, refuses a duplicated pair, records a capacity reject and leaves one missing. The reference run logs each hit with whether ingestion, reduction, the trigger pass, the extraction or a review (including a candidate still owed its review) was outstanding; each repetition kills at one of those hits (first, middle, last), the killed store must differ from the reference, and the restarted store must equal it (jobs, applications, operations, decisions, claim heads with strength/basis/verdicts, review outcomes, batches, review requests and priorities, triggers, trigger cursor). The Task 5–8 matrices ran at 3 repetitions in the full runs.

- Defects the matrices found (fixed, regression-tested): a supersession in the same application wrote a newer candidate revision of its replacement, which then was never scheduled for review (`test_a_superseding_replacement_is_reviewed_at_its_latest_revision`); and the Task 7 request view stripped every review assessment, so any request consulting active reviewed guidance failed validation (`request_invalid`) — latent until claims could become active (`test_active_reviewed_claims_stay_valid_in_later_requests`).
- Verification of the tests themselves (deliberate mutations, each reverted; script in the session scratchpad): 27 mutations. 23 were caught at once, among them: observed claims with any wording, regions ignored, no blockquote rule, LF-only line splitting, any user span as a declaration, partitioned messages as declarations, strength counting events instead of classes, strength ignoring contradictions, no duplicate-verdict check, foreign batches accepted, capacity rejects archiving, assessments re-scheduling review, candidate revisions in the review read set, earlier assessments shown to the reviewer, no input partitioning, no deferral, trigger time from processing time, null families matching, no late resolution, no scope check, unreviewed replacements superseding active guidance, exit-code mappings on prose locators. Three first slipped past and were caught after the tests were strengthened: the snapshot-digest check (every stale case also moved the revision; `test_assessment_is_bound_to_the_snapshot_digest`), counting non-relevant events toward the debounce (no non-relevant event sat between a failure and its success), and taking the first rather than the latest cited failure (lessons cited one failure). The crash-ordering defect — review jobs scheduled in a separate transaction after the application commits — passes every single-process test and is caught only by the matrix (`job.after_complete`); it first slipped past the matrix too, because its outstanding-work probe did not count a candidate still owed a review, so that window was never chosen as a kill point.

Named synthetic variants: prompt texts with fenced code, blockquotes, attachments, email and diff blocks, tracebacks, logs, terminal output, indented code, CRLF and a 77 KB partitioned message; reruns under new tool-use IDs, changed commands (`-x`, a pipeline for a null family) and passing results on failing commands; a second host session; controlled capture times; a second registered workspace row (scope test; attachment itself is full-release work); a memory-delivered telemetry event through the real normalization. Verdicts equal the recorded ones, so `docs/host-compatibility.json` was not rewritten. **Unavailable:** the plan's extraction/admission slices of the 12-scenario smoke subset, the ACCEPTANCE_TESTS §5 extraction corpus and quality gates (precision, recall, failed-approach recall, quiet-case caution), live review quality (scoped wording, semantic A13) and the 180-second admission target with a real provider.
- Review focus recheck: **(1) same source event twice across a crash** — holds for everything built so far; the new matrix shows one admission, one review schedule, one assessment application and one trigger pass per event across kills at every Task 9 write boundary; duplicate proposals and unchanged reruns add no support or strength. Task 10 revalidation outcomes pending. **(2) quoted or repeated through agent memory** — holds for Task 8 lineage and now Task 9: quoted, pasted, attached, email/diff and machine-output spans never become declarations; host-generated and partitioned text neither; repeated declarations stay one authority class (never above supported); retrieval and delivery telemetry, repetition and stated self-confidence never raise strength. - Gaps and limits:

  - **No complete-dependency domain** is certified, so real corroboration needs a changed invocation, another artifact snapshot or a distinct source.
  - **Completion statements** are not semantically reviewed; **`config.exact_value`** has no parser.
  - **Schema:** schema-4 development stores are refused, never migrated (Task 17).

- CI: **run 45 (56b8783)** failed on all five jobs, in only `test_claim_admission_crash_matches_reference`, at the "kill must land before the work is complete" guard: the outstanding-work probe counted an assessment-only candidate revision (revise, capacity — by design never re-reviewed) as still owed a review, so the final completion hit was misclassified and a kill landed after all work was done. Reproduced locally in the first 20-repetition run, root-caused and fixed in the test probe (match owed reviews by claim, not revision), which still catches the crash-ordering mutation. **Run 46 (629e15d): all five jobs green.** Linux 3.12, 3.13 and 3.14 passed (3.13 also passed the requirements-lock check and offline provisioning). macOS 3.13 passed 600 with 12 skipped. Windows 3.13 passed 557 with 55 skipped: the Task 9 admission, review and debounce tests and the new kill/restart matrix (3 reps per failpoint) ran natively, and the runner had no orphan processes to clean up.

- Behavior: see decision 0012. In short: every reason to revalidate is one idempotent `claim_rechecks` row; FailureRecheck (a certified success of a known matching action family after the lesson's latest supporting failure in certified order) and a changed dependency (certified writes, environment observations; transitively through claim dependencies; a preserved fingerprint does nothing) set `needs_revalidation`, attach the observation as `context`, advance the guidance epoch and queue a coalesced protected revalidation in the ingestion transaction, before any model call; a candidate defers them to its activation; a revalidation result settles a recheck only when its read set names the observation and the still-current revision, and a plain keep never restores a failure lesson's caution after a later success; `revalidate.guidance` caps caution on the dependency gate and freshness while keeping contextual recall; an inferred replacement of active guidance is held and applied in the replacement's activation transaction; the planner settles model-free revalidations, schedules due reviews, groups consolidation candidates (conflicts first, content-keyed against loops) and opens one question per conflict of user declarations.
- Wired now versus consumer hooks: **wired** — FailureRecheck, dependency and conflict rechecks at ingestion, deferred rechecks, revalidation settling and its outcomes, the advice gate, dependency fingerprint resolution, deterministic review classes and due reviews, held supersessions, merge strength, consolidation groups and jobs, intent questions, `revalidate_dependents`. **Consumer hooks for later tasks** — Task 11 composes `revalidate.guidance` into packets, adds the hook-side live dependency check, renders open `intent_questions` as one conflict item and labels candidates and pending revalidation; Task 12a's guarded `correct`/`declare` transports reuse the review, held-supersession and recheck paths unchanged; Task 12b deduplicates questions per session and fences delivery on the guidance epoch these changes advance; Tasks 15/16 make the new tables participate in retention and forgetting; a `config` fingerprint resolver and detection of file changes outside captured tools (a branch checkout, an editor) remain later work.
- Commands (Linux x86_64 container, SQLite 3.45.1, Claude Code CLI 2.1.302 installed; fixtures pinned to 2.1.301):

  - `UV_PROJECT_ENVIRONMENT=<scratch> uv run --locked --python 3.12 python -m pytest -q -rs` → exit 0, 641 passed, 13 skipped; the same on 3.14 → exit 0, 641 passed, 13 skipped.
  - As `nobody` via `runuser` with a temporary `/var/tmp` basetemp (removed afterwards): the `-k permission` capture tests → 5 passed; `tests/integration/test_consolidation.py tests/integration/test_claim_admission.py tests/platform/test_worker_lifecycle.py tests/integration/test_operations.py` → 102 passed, 2 skipped (Windows-only breakaway, 2.1.301 installed host).
  - `RECALITH_CRASH_REPS=20 .venv/bin/python -m pytest -q tests/integration/test_worker.py -k "revision_crash or lineage"` → exit 0, 23 passed in 804 s: the new matrix (`recheck.after_mark`, `revalidate.after_settle`, `ingest.before_commit`, `ingest.after_commit`, `review.after_assessment`, `review.after_schedule` and the seven `failpoints.WORKER` points × 20) and the updated Task 8 merge matrix (10 points × 20, `planner.after_schedule` included). The revision scenario reduces the real lifecycle with an admitted failure lesson on `test_mid` carrying a code-resolved file dependency, a fact depending on it and a lesson on `test_long` still under review, and spools a passing `test_mid` rerun, a `Write` of the dependency file and a passing `test_long` rerun; the reference run logs each hit with whether ingestion, reduction, a recheck (recorded, or owed: a matching success or dependency write no recheck names yet), a revalidation or a review was outstanding; every repetition kills at one of those hits (first, middle, last), the killed store must differ from the reference and the restarted store must equal it (jobs, applications, operations, claims with status, freshness, strength, retry conditions and context, rechecks with outcomes, revision audits, artifact writes, guidance epoch). The Task 5–9 matrices ran at 3 repetitions in the full runs.
  - `tools/generate_schemas.py --check`, `tools/generate_hook_wire.py --check` → exit 0; `uvx ruff@0.14.0 check --select F,E9,B src/recalith tests/integration/test_consolidation.py tests/integration/consolidation_scenarios.py` → only the pre-existing `fast/redact.py` B905; `requirements.lock` unchanged (no dependency change).
- Verification of the tests themselves (deliberate mutations, each reverted; scripts in the session scratchpad): 35 mutations. 30 were caught at once, among them: an older replay rechecking, the family ignoring the working directory, a recheck disputing the claim, no freshness change, no context link, candidates revised directly instead of deferred, no transitive marking, no dependency-preserving check, settling without the observation in the read set, a keep clearing a later success, no due-date bump, the dependency gate hiding recall, caution ignoring the dependency gate or freshness, post-evidence writes ignored, a model fingerprint trusted, review classes without cycle detection or claim dependencies, consolidation keyed by revision numbers (a loop), disjoint conditions ignored, a repeated question, due reviews changing freshness, unreviewed supersession, activation without the held supersession, no deferred activation, the invalidation and declaration gates removed, no conflict suspension, and merge revalidations needing a model. Three first slipped past and were caught after the tests were strengthened: capture time trusted for non-hook events (`test_capture_time_orders_only_live_hooks`), a keep clearing an unresolved source conflict (the conflict test now adds a dependency change and a keep), and consolidation without conflict priority (`test_consolidation_prioritizes_current_conflicts`, more cosmetic groups than the bound). Two crash-ordering defects pass every single-process test (31/31) and are caught only by the matrix: the revalidation job enqueued in a second transaction after the ingestion commit (`ingest.after_commit`: the replayed segment is a duplicate, so the job is lost), and FailureRecheck/dependency marking moved to a second transaction (`ingest.after_commit` and `recheck.after_mark`). In both, the probe's spooled-segment term already selects the kill window; the owed-recheck term is kept so a defect that unlinks the segment first is still chosen.
- Acceptance (all `in_progress`, none passed): **A18**, **A19** (Task 10 part), **A20**, **A21**, **A22**, **A43**, **A44**, **A51**, **A62** and **A09** (claim-level suspension) with the tests in the manifest. These are deterministic mechanics over real payloads with recorded proposals.
- Real versus synthetic inputs: real 2.1.301 payloads through the real adapter: lifecycle (prompts, the failing `pytest -q test_mid.py` and `pytest -q test_long.py`, the passing `pytest -q -s test_pass.py` result, `Write`, `echo`). Named synthetic variants: reruns of the failing commands whose result is the real passing result under new tool-use IDs; the same in a second host session or another package directory (`cwd`); the same command through a pipeline (inexact parse); a `Write` of another workspace path; `git merge feature/x` over the real `echo` result; a conflicting `PostToolUseFailure` payload under the same tool call; controlled capture times equal to, before or after the failure's; prompt texts for user declarations; synthetic envelopes without a working directory or with a transcript origin (unit-level calls); workspace dependency files written by the tests. Model behavior is recorded proposals through the real persisted path.
- Live versus unavailable (B1 still open; this session's credentials and proxy were never used as a route): `python tools/probe_provider.py --config probe-private/local-config.json --output <scratch>/hc.json` → exit 4 (`unavailable`; blocking `auth_route`, `live_launch`, `operator_attestation`, `schema_output`) and `python tools/check_provider_runtime.py --config probe-private/local-config.json --output <scratch>/hc.json` → exit 4 (clean and host-marked isolation pass; route `route_uncertified`, `selected_auth_missing`), on the same Claude Code 2.1.302 executable (SHA-256 `60d7eb7f…`); the ignored local config was recreated from the tracked example in request-capture mode only, with a placeholder model and no credentials. Verdicts equal the recorded ones, so `docs/host-compatibility.json` was not rewritten. **Unavailable:** live revalidation quality, the ACCEPTANCE_TESTS §5 revision scenarios and gates (changed conditions, legitimate retries, conflicting evidence, explicit corrections; "legitimate retry incorrectly discouraged ≤ 5%", conflict/uncertainty preservation ≥ 95%) and the revision slices of the 12-scenario smoke subset.
- Review focus recheck: **(1) same source event twice across a crash** — holds for everything built so far, now including revision: one recheck per claim, reason and observation, one revision, one revalidation job and one settlement across kills at every Task 10 write boundary (new matrix); a replayed hook or session is a duplicate event and rechecks nothing (`test_replay_keeps_history_and_withdrawn_guidance`, `test_old_or_unknown_order_success_does_not_assert_contradiction`). **(2) quoted or repeated through agent memory** — holds: counter-observations are context only, a revalidation's self-citation adds no support class (`test_derived_writes_do_not_loop`), and no Task 10 path assigns authority or strength except lineage. **(3) correction/forget before the final delivery fence** — still pending (Tasks 12b/16); every recheck, settlement, held supersession and question advances the guidance epoch the fence will use. **(4) paths** — dependency keys are portable workspace paths fingerprinted through `PathPolicy`; identical argv in another package directory is another family; Windows/macOS behavior comes from the CI runners. **(5) provider or host version change** — FailureRecheck and dependency changes need certified tool authority, which an uncertified host version never gets; the route recheck is unchanged.
- Gaps and limits:
  - **B1** blocks live revalidation; production preview has no backend, so revalidation, review and consolidation jobs wait (`provider_unavailable`) while FailureRecheck, dependency marking, deferral, settling of model-free jobs and questions still work deterministically.
  - **Certified order** for Claude 2.1.301 is the live hook capture time; hooks captured in the same microsecond are `order_unknown`; transcript-recovered or imported results never order.
  - **File changes outside captured tools** (checkout, editor) are not observed as dependency changes; the live check at delivery (Task 11) still compares current bytes. A file changed between the evidence and admission outside a captured tool is attributed to the admission-time bytes. `config` dependencies stay unknown.
  - **Source conflicts** keep their claims at `needs_revalidation` until a resolution path exists; nothing resolves `source_conflicts` rows yet.
  - **Schema:** schema-5 development stores are refused, never migrated (Task 17).
  - **Crash protocol:** process death, not power loss.
- CI: **run 50 (3d12ed3)**: Linux 3.12, 3.13 and 3.14 and macOS 3.13 green; Windows 3.13 failed only the Task 4 latency gate `test_idle_poll_state_available_within_five_seconds` (typed-state p95 5.53 s, max 5.89 s, gate 5 s). This is not a Task 10 regression: **run 47** (Task 9's docs-only commit, Task 9 code) failed the same test on Windows with p95 5.84 s and max 6.98 s, a failure the Task 9 checkpoint did not record. Locally the Task 10 planner sweep costs 0.12 ms and an idle tick 0.4 ms, and the test passes on Linux even under eight busy CPU loops (p95 4.37 s), so CPU contention does not reproduce it. Root cause, as far as it can be established without a Windows machine: the worst case is `5 s − arrival phase + time from the next poll's start to the completed ingest job`, and the idle wait ran *after* the tick and the status write, so a slow Windows poll (fsync'd transactions, the atomic status file) lengthened every idle cycle by its own duration. The fix (no gate weakened): the worker's 1 s/5 s cadence now runs from poll start (`Worker.cadence_wait`, `test_poll_cadence_runs_from_poll_start`), and worker status reports `last_poll_seconds`, which the latency test prints per trial, so a remaining overshoot on Windows can be attributed. **Run 51 (7cd1b01): all five jobs green.** Linux 3.12, 3.13 and 3.14 passed (3.13 also passed the requirements-lock check and offline provisioning); macOS 3.13 passed; Windows 3.13 passed 600 with 55 skipped, running the Task 10 consolidation tests, the revision kill/restart matrix and the updated merge matrix (3 reps per failpoint) natively, and the runner had no orphan processes to clean up. The latency gate passed on Windows this time; since it failed only intermittently before (runs 47 and 50 of the last five), one green run does not prove the overshoot is gone: watch the printed `poll_seconds` if it recurs.

### 2026-10-19 — Task 11: retrieval and context composition

Changed: `controller/worker.py` (the projection step each poll after the planner), `capture/provenance.py` (`resolve_in`/`environment_values_in`: resolution inside a caller's snapshot), `fast/failpoints.py` (`failpoints.RETRIEVAL`: `index.after_refresh`, `views.before_commit`, `views.after_publish`), `fast/wire.py` + `tools/generate_hook_wire.py` (generated validators for `ContextQuery`/`ContextPacket`/`ContextItem` with stdlib twins of the item and packet rules; wire tables regenerated; the strict schemas are unchanged), `storage/db.py` (`SCHEMA_VERSION = 7`), `docs/host-compatibility.json` (provider sections re-probed on 2.1.303). Docs: decision 0013, DATA_CONTRACTS §2.3/§9/§10/§11, SYSTEM_SPEC §8.1/§8.2, IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN Task 11 files, coverage manifest.
- Behavior: see decision 0013. In short: the worker keeps a disposable lexical/structural index (FTS5 when detected, else the token index; same tokenizer) in bounded transactions after commits, and readers cover its lag from live heads; status and workspace/task scope are excluded on hot columns, then full tri-state applicability runs, before any cap; candidates come from lexical and structural matches (exact action family, paths, tools, subjects), the bound task (scope or support bound to it), claim-dependency neighbors, open questions and the task's own state, episodes and latest certified results; claims are decided by Task 10's `revalidate.guidance` plus an action-specific match for caution; every §8.2 row is implemented (tentative candidates and weak claims only for the bound task or an exact action; pending revalidation labeled with its reasons; disputes as one conflict item with both sides; an open intent question as one conflict item with its declarations folded in; expired/redacted support, by field or live payload state, omitted); ranking is deterministic; packets are budgeted on the exact rendered text (`ceil(bytes/3)`; 2,000/1,000/400/2,000, manual 2,000, one shared 400 substitution for an included caution, 1,400 total, caller maximum binding) and fenced on a fresh snapshot. The worker republishes bounded per-workspace/task/purpose views and up to 128 fallback rows when an epoch moves, in one snapshot with the epochs it renders. The stdlib hook compiler serves a view only at equal epochs, else at most 32 live-filtered fallback rows (head revision and status, available support evidence, no newer tombstone), evaluates conditions with a stdlib twin (PEP 440 only via a precomputed result for the exact consulted value), runs the bounded live dependency check (16 files, 1 MiB, 50 ms, under the shared deadline), and validates the packet at a fresh fence; it never writes, waits or repairs.
- Wired now versus consumer hooks: **wired** — `retrieve`, `compile_context` (service, strict DTOs), the projection step, views and fallback rows, `fast.context.hook_compile_context`/`fast.cache.compile_packet` and `fast.fence.check` (stdlib), the packet-level fence, bounded live dependency checks, the §8.2 labels, questions, recent observations. **Consumer hooks for later tasks** — Task 12a exposes retrieval through the public service/CLI; Task 12b adds the delivery branch of `handle_hook` (session reservation, stdout emission, `hook_record_delivery`/`record_delivery` receipts, suppression keys with condition digests, per-session question deduplication, the session overlay invalidating older comparisons, supplying `branch_ref`, the query environment and `substitute_pre_action`) and bounds in-flight emission after the fence; Tasks 15/16 purge the projection rows with the records they render and advance the guidance epoch for evidence expiry (the compiler already omits expired support); Task 17 reports projection lag in health; Task 13 certifies real delivery.

- Gaps and limits:

  - **Views** cover each workspace and its four most recently active tasks; other tasks fall back (`view_missing`) to workspace rows; candidate order among equal scores follows record IDs.

  - **Hook environment** is the view's published environment unless the query supplies one (Task 12b); `config` dependencies stay unknown.
  - **Schema:** schema-6 development stores are refused, never migrated (Task 17).
  - **Crash protocol:** process death, not power loss.

### 2026-10-19 — Task 12a: public service and CLI contracts (commit 8e267d2)

- Files: new `src/recalith/service.py`, `src/recalith/cli/{__init__,main,operator_guard,inspect,mutate}.py`; `src/recalith/__main__.py` now hands over to `cli.main.main` (the interim every-command `capability_unavailable` stub is gone). Changed: `capture/pipeline.py` (`ingest_in_transaction`: the per-segment ingestion body, reused unchanged by the guarded writes; `ingest_ready` calls it), `controller/apply.py` (`apply_in_transaction(..., review_origin=)` so a correction's replacement review is recorded as `operator_correction`), `controller/jobs.py` (`retry` of a `failed` job), `core/identity.py` (`operator_verifiers`/`operator_revoked` tables in `local.toml`, `initialize_installation(..., operator_verifiers=)` also when completing an interrupted init, `operator_tokens`/`update_operator_tokens`), `fast/failpoints.py` (`service.after_event`, `service.after_commit`, `init.after_store` join `failpoints.SERVICE`). Tests: `tests/integration/test_cli.py` (152 cases), builders `tests/integration/cli_scenarios.py`, the kill/restart matrix `test_service_write_crash_matches_reference` (`tests/integration/test_worker.py`, driver `worker_scenarios.py`), layering audits `test_service_and_domain_import_no_cli_or_host_entry` and `test_no_domain_module_names_the_cli` (`tests/platform/test_hook_imports.py`). Docs: decision 0014, DATA_CONTRACTS §11/§12, IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN Task 12a files, coverage manifest. **No schema change** (no `0008.sql`): verifiers live in `local.toml` as SYSTEM_SPEC §13.1 says, refusal audits go through the spool, job retry uses existing tables.
- Behavior: see decision 0014. In short: every DATA_CONTRACTS §12 form parses exactly (syntax errors exit 2) and prints one JSON object with `--json`; the operator guard inspects the original caller before any actor exists — any host marker (`CLAUDECODE`, `CLAUDE_CODE_CHILD_SESSION`) or worker/curator role marker refuses (6) even with a terminal and a valid token, with the external-OS-terminal route and no override; a supplied token file must be a regular, private, owned, strict `OperatorTokenFile` (else 6, never 2, never a terminal fallback); token create/revoke need the terminal; other administrative forms need the terminal (POSIX: stdin is a terminal and a controlling terminal exists; Windows: a console input handle) or a token matching an active local verifier. Only then is the operator actor assigned. Refusals spool a content-free `agent_attempted_operator_command` (command kind, caller category, reason) when an installation exists, report `audit: unavailable` when that fails and still refuse, and create nothing before init. `declare` records the exact statement as an `operator_declaration` and a weak `user_declared` candidate whose review is queued (band 1, `typed_declaration`); `correct` invalidates the expected current target, advances the guidance epoch and queues the replacement's review (`operator_correction`) in one transaction with no model call; both run the ingestion helper and `apply_in_transaction` unchanged and are idempotent by content (a re-run returns `duplicate`). `observe` is an untrusted `generic_observation` (7 when not durably captured); `context` is `compile_context` (8 when degraded); `status`/`doctor` exit 8 with `health_composition_unavailable` and `autonomous_ready: false`; later-stage forms pass the guard and return 4 naming their owner.
- Wired now versus consumer hooks: **wired** — the service and CLI for every §12 form, the guard, pre-init tokens and verifiers, guarded `declare`/`correct`/`undo --apply`/`jobs retry`/`worker start|stop|run --mode persistent`/`workspace rebind|discard-copy`/`init`, read-only `status`/`doctor`/`observe`/`context`/`search`/`show`/`tasks|episodes|claims`/`jobs list`/`undo --preview`. **Consumer hooks for later tasks** — Task 12b: the delivery branch of `handle_hook`, `record_delivery`/`hook_record_delivery`, receipts, suppression keys, per-session question deduplication, session-overlay invalidation, supplying `branch_ref`, the query environment and `substitute_pre_action`, and bounding in-flight emission after the fence (corrections now commit through the guarded transport; their delivery-side fence is 12b). Task 13: `integrate` (and `--remove`), host command-pattern deny rules for the administrative spellings, certified marker inheritance on the real host and a live operator workflow. Tasks 15/16: `backup`, `restore`, `forget` behind the existing guarded forms. Task 17: health composition (status stops reporting `health_composition_unavailable`), `doctor` probes. Full release: export/import/Breadcrumbs, upgrade, project fork, workspace attach, session worker mode, `doctor --full-integrity`, an MCP transport over `recalith.service` (without `declare`/`correct`).
- Commands (Linux x86_64 container, SQLite 3.45.1, Claude Code CLI 2.1.303 installed; fixtures pinned to 2.1.301):

- Gaps and limits:
  - **B1** blocks live review quality and every live operator check.
  - **Typed-declaration proposition keys** are code-derived from the exact text: two differently worded typed declarations are not grouped (no automatic intent question between them).
  - **Identical retries:** re-declaring identical text after its candidate was rejected returns the earlier result; a reworded statement is a new declaration.
  - **Windows terminal:** only the console-input-handle rule is implemented and exercised (a real new console in CI); MSYS/mintty pipes count as noninteractive.
  - **`local.toml` updates** (token register/revoke) are atomic replaces without a cross-process lock; two concurrent operator token commands could lose one update.
  - **Guard scope:** same-user friction, not authentication; Task 13's command-pattern rules are not installed yet.
  - **Crash protocol:** process death, not power loss.

Root cause, a test defect: on Windows the `subprocess.Popen` audit event carries the command-line *string*, which the probe iterated character by character (the Task 4 audit already handled this); the product spawned the right internal entry. The probe now records the string and asserts on it. The Windows console variant of the guard (`test_marked_ide_tty_plus_token_still_refused` and a quarter of the refusal matrix) ran natively and passed. The Windows job took 49 minutes (about 16 more than before, from ~350 extra CLI child processes). The Windows typed-state latency gate passed, and the runner listed no orphan processes.

- Behavior: see decision 0015. In short: delivery runs only on a manifest-installed path for a certified host version (2.1.301; the installed 2.1.303 would be `host_version_uncertified`), after capture and before the worker start; it compiles (Task 11 views or ≤32 fallback rows), takes the per-session lock (≤20 ms; `flock`/`msvcrt.locking`), reads scratch and the durable receipts read-only, resolves dead reservations to uncertain (still charged), suppresses by `(record_id, revision, condition_digest, purpose_class)` from emitted entries and ingested emitted receipts, adds the overlay's typed failures and lets a later success or changed dependency path downgrade an older caution, fits the packet caps and the 4,000 unchanged-memory allowance (cautions, conflicts and changed revisions may exceed it; restorations charged separately; one shared ≤400 substitution when pre-action injection is not installed), **reserves** in scratch, runs the **fresh final fence**, writes and flushes the `hookSpecificOutput.additionalContext` envelope, records `in_flight_change` from a fresh snapshot after the flush, spools the `memory_delivered` receipt, marks the entry emitted. The worker turns receipts into one `deliveries` row per attempt in the ingestion transaction (emitted upgrades uncertain, never the reverse; guidance epoch unchanged) and links observed follow-ups (a certified test result after a pre-action delivery; `acknowledged` for an assistant message citing an emitted record — never support or success).
- Wired now versus later tasks: **wired** — the delivery branch for session start (workspace-only lookup without a binding), prompt, pre-action and compaction restoration (once per cycle; prompt fallback without the compact path), scratch reservations/totals/eviction/TTL, condition-aware suppression and per-session question deduplication, the overlay at delivery, the final fence with in-flight recording, `hook_record_delivery`, the service `record_delivery`, receipt accounting and outcome links, `settle` at session end. **Later tasks** — Task 13: `integrate` writing certified `delivery_paths`, the nonce protocol against this engine for all four paths, host command deny rules; Tasks 15/16: receipts and outcome links in retention/backup, forgetting purging `deliveries`/`outcome_links` rows and scratch files of forgotten records (the tombstone epoch already clears overlay content); Task 17: delivery states, degradations, uncertain attempts and `in_flight_change` counts in health; the hook allowance reading a configured budget instead of the 4,000 default.

- Review focus recheck (Task 12b's part): **(1) same source event twice across a crash** — a receipt's key names its attempt and state, so a re-spooled receipt is a duplicate event; one row per attempt; the delivery matrix shows one accounted charge per attempt after kills at every delivery and receipt-ingestion boundary. **(2) quoted or repeated through agent memory** — receipts are memory-channel, derived, rootless telemetry; outcome links never add support, roots, strength or task success; an acknowledging assistant message is labeled `acknowledged` only. **(3) correction/forget before the final delivery fence** — owned here on the delivery side: the fence runs after the reservation and immediately before the write; a guarded `recalith correct` committed before it suppresses the old text and the reservation is withdrawn; one committed after it is recorded as in flight (`fenced_at`, `emitted_at`, `in_flight_change`) and never claimed retracted; forgetting (Task 16) is fenced by the same tombstone epoch and clears overlay content. **(4) paths** — the branch is read from `<workspace>/.git/HEAD` only (no-follow, bounded; worktrees unknown); scratch lives under the installation; Windows locking (`msvcrt`) is exercised by the Windows CI job, not claimed from POSIX. **(5) provider or host version change** — delivery requires a certified host version: the installed 2.1.303 is `host_version_uncertified` (conservative capture); **no fake delivery claim**: local emission into a test manifest is not certification, A27 stays mechanics-only.

  - **B1** blocks every live delivery and recall check.
  - **Allowance**: the hook uses the specified 4,000 default (it reads no `project.toml`); a configured budget needs a hook-readable copy (later).
  - **Restoration**: an auto-compaction without `PreCompact` (not observed headless) opens no new cycle and is not restored.
  - **Resume lag**: a session resumed after its scratch was discarded but before its last receipts were ingested undercounts until ingestion.
  - **Outcome labels** are telemetry; `challenged`/`action_changed` are not assigned automatically.
  - **Schema:** schema-7 development stores are refused, never migrated (Task 17).
  - **Crash protocol:** process death, not power loss.

- Behavior: see decision 0016. In short: pins cover every live owner's whole evidence chain (current `candidate`/`active`/`disputed` claims with supports, contradictions and context; `active`/`paused` non-linked tasks with their revision's evidence and their open working range; `open` episodes; record revisions transitively, so an archived intermediary keeps a live claim's chain; derivation parents and roots; forwarded subagent results) plus owed work (unreduced events, inputs of unfinished jobs, pending recheck observations, undebounced failure triggers). An available payload strictly older than its window by `recorded_at` (180 days unpinned, 90 days for delivery receipts; the installation's `RetentionConfig` decides) expires at day 181: candidates are selected in a read snapshot, the pin closure is recomputed inside the expiry transaction and every candidate rechecked there (`repinned` when a concurrent reference won), the payload and conflict bodies about it are dropped, a content-free `retention_expiry` audit is written and the guidance epoch advances. A concurrent new reference either commits first and pins, or fails `check_link` (`evidence_unavailable` anywhere on its lineage chain). Releasing the last owner makes an already-old payload expire at the next sweep. Retention alone lowers nothing; when support is actually gone (the forgetting override `release_payloads`, an undo reactivating a claim over expired evidence, the sweep's backstop) the claim is downgraded in that transaction (strength from the support left, `evidence_availability` `expired`/`redacted`, `needs_revalidation`, a `support_lost` recheck and revalidation job, the epoch). Delivery rows and outcome links age out after 90 days, terminal jobs' proposal bodies after 7; abandoned scratch that still owes receipts is settled through the spool before it is pruned. Backups are a directory of `memory.sqlite` (the online backup interface in one step), `ledger.json` (that snapshot's tombstone ledger) and `manifest.json` (digests), journaled, staged, verified, renamed into place, recorded and followed by the latest ledger copy (`.recalith/state/ledger.json`); recovery finishes or removes interrupted backups; verified backups older than 30 days are deleted (files, then row; never a location that no longer names that backup). `recalith backup --output PATH` runs behind the guard; `recalith restore PATH --expected-digest D` verifies first (a backup that does not verify is exit 2 with its reason; a verified one answers 4 naming Task 16) and never touches the live store. `RetentionSummary` reports pinned/unpinned payload bytes, live owners, free disk and pinned payloads past their window (`deferred_compaction_count`); the last sweep's report is in worker status and `recalith status`.
- Wired now versus later: **wired** — everything above. **Task 16** — `apply_forget` calling `release_payloads`, tombstones and the latest-ledger update at forget, purging projection rows (`retrieval_docs`, `lexical_index`, `lexical_fts`, `context_views`), `deliveries`/`outcome_links` rows and scratch files of forgotten records, deleting affected older backups and the post-forget backup through `backup.create`, and the apply phase of restore (maintenance, newer ledger first, suppression before serving). **Task 17** — `RetentionSummary`, backup and retention state composed into `HealthReport`. **Full release** — capsules (compaction of pinned payloads past their window), export/import, the Breadcrumbs importer; their A35/A38/A39/A42/A55/A71 variants stay pending. **Open in Task 15** — age pruning of derived revision bodies (`episode_after_closure` 180 days, `closed_task_history` 90 days) is not built (SYSTEM_SPEC §11 sets it; the plan's preview core does not list it).
- Commands (Linux x86_64 container, SQLite 3.45.1, Claude Code CLI 2.1.304 installed; fixtures pinned to 2.1.301):
  - Tests first, against the pre-implementation tree: `.venv/bin/python -m pytest -q tests/integration/test_retention.py` → exit 2, 1 collection error (`recalith.privacy` absent).
  - `.venv/bin/python -m pytest -q -rs` (Python 3.13.16) → exit 0, 911 passed, 14 skipped (Task 12b's 14 skips; the retention matrix ran at 3 repetitions).
  - `UV_PROJECT_ENVIRONMENT=<scratch> uv run --locked --python 3.12 python -m pytest -q -rs` → exit 0, 911 passed, 14 skipped; the same on **3.14** → exit 0, 911 passed, 14 skipped.
  - `RECALITH_CRASH_REPS=20 .venv/bin/python -m pytest -q tests/integration/test_worker.py -k retention_backup_crash` → exit 0, 8 passed in 497 s: `retention.after_select`, `retention.before_commit`, `retention.after_commit`, `backup.after_snapshot`, `backup.after_manifest`, `backup.after_publish`, `backup.after_record`, `backup.after_prune` × 20 kill/restart runs. The scenario is the full owner set over real payloads plus a synthetic tombstone; the script takes a backup 40 days before day 181, runs the worker at day 181 (a clock ahead of every capture, no provider) and takes a backup at day 181. A plain reference run gives the outcome (30 of 57 payloads expired, one expiry audit, the aged backup deleted, the new one verified with the ledger and the expiry in its snapshot); a counting run of a fresh build logs each hit with whether work was owed, judged from the effects (a payload the reference expired still present, the aged backup on disk or not marked deleted, the new backup missing, unrecorded or failing the test's own hashing, a staging directory left, the ledger copy behind the store's epoch), never from `retention_state`; each repetition kills at an owed hit (first, middle, last), the killed state must differ, no output may exist that does not verify, no pinned payload may be gone and no claim changed, and after the operator re-runs the incomplete commands and the worker drains the state equals the reference.
