- Verification of the tests themselves (26 deliberate mutations, each reverted; script in the session scratchpad; per mutation `tests/integration/test_retention.py` plus the CLI unavailable-forms test, and the retention matrix at 3 reps). All 26 are caught in the end. **Caught at once by single-process tests (20):** pins taken outside the expiry transaction (only the race test), pinned payloads expired, forwarded results ignored, undebounced triggers ignored, retention lowering strength without lost support, expiry without the epoch advance, the day-180 boundary off by one, admission ignoring the chain, strength counting lost support, undo reviving lost support, the hook pruning owed scratch, the worker pruning without settling, delivery metadata never aging, a backup without the ledger, verification skipping file digests or the schema, a backup replacing an existing output, retention deleting a foreign location, the journal written after the snapshot, recovery forgetting a published backup. **Only the matrix catches** the crash-ordering defect *the sweep time recorded before the sweep's work* (a kill before the expiry commits leaves the restarted worker thinking the sweep is done; every single-process test passes). **Five first slipped past and the tests were strengthened:** ignoring transitive record links and ignoring a link's named roots (each masked the other: the transitive claim also named its root; it now cites the intermediary without roots, and a synthetic state removes the intermediary's own links so only named roots pin), ignoring a task's open working range (the paused task's revision cited the same events; its session start and call start, cited by no revision, are now asserted), ignoring unfinished jobs (the owed events were also pending triggers; an extraction naming an otherwise unowned prompt is now asserted pinned, then released when cancelled), and a snapshot by plain file copy (closing the last connection checkpoints the WAL, so a copy was complete in tests; the consistency test now holds a connection open, as the worker does, and requires every commit made before the backup). The matrix also catches pinned payloads expired, forwarded results ignored, strength lowered, transitive links ignored, a backup without the ledger, the journal written late and recovery forgetting a published backup.
- Acceptance (all `in_progress`, none passed): **A35** preview variant (the 11 retention tests and the matrix; the forget path end to end is Task 16), **A40** preview variant (corrupt, tampered, incomplete and schema-forged backups rejected through the real CLI with the live store byte-for-byte unchanged; a live store of another schema refuses the backup write; Task 17 owns the rest), **A21** and **A05** add the matrix and the replay test. **A36/A37** gain the backup parts they build on (ledger in every backup, the latest ledger copy, verification before restore, the pin override) without being claimed. The A35 full-only capsule variant and every transfer/capsule case stay pending/unavailable.
- Real versus synthetic inputs: real 2.1.301 payloads through the real adapter for every event (lifecycle and subagent sessions); claims admitted through the real extraction/review path with recorded proposals; the typed declaration and the correction through the guarded service as an operator; task completion through a recorded `update_task` citing the user's statement; a dispute and archives through recorded revalidation decisions; backups through the real guarded `recalith` child process with an explicitly provisioned fixture token. Named synthetic variants: a second host session, prompt texts (declarations, completion statements), the resume `SessionStart` in ended sessions, reruns of real calls (the passing pytest inside another subagent with the real Agent forwarding it, six `seq` reruns for the race), a changed `Edit` result under an expired event's source key, synthetic-adapter experiment events whose shared tokens merge two classes. Synthetic store states: an open episode (the fixtures never reach a 64-step flush) and its settling, tombstone rows with their epoch (Task 16 owns forgetting), a seeded claim with a record link and the intermediary's links removed, corrupted, resealed and schema-forged backup copies, an older-schema live store. Controlled time: events keep real capture times; sweeps take an explicit `now` (newest capture + N days) and the worker a clock ahead of every capture.
- Live versus unavailable (B1 still open; this session's credentials and proxy were never used as a route): the installed CLI changed to **Claude Code 2.1.304** (executable SHA-256 `ea0f1357…`, was 2.1.303 `375988f4…`). With the ignored local config recreated from the tracked example (no model, `not_configured` route, unchecked attestation, request capture and live modes, no credentials): `python tools/probe_provider.py --config probe-private/local-config.json --output <scratch>/hc.json` → exit 4 (`unavailable`; blocking `auth_route`, `live_launch`, `operator_attestation`, `schema_output`; host version, tool inventory, customization absence, environment isolation and bounds pass) and `python tools/check_provider_runtime.py ...` → exit 4 (clean and host-marked isolation pass; `route_uncertified`, `selected_auth_missing`). Only the host version and digest changed (and the non-isolated control's model-visible tool count, 22 → 12; the isolated runs still expose no tools), so the provider sections of `docs/host-compatibility.json` were rewritten with the 2.1.304 observation; other sections untouched. Retention and backup need no model. **Unavailable:** live recall quality, live delivery (the nonce protocol), live review/revalidation quality, live operator workflows on a real IDE host, and every live certification.
- Review focus recheck (Task 15's part): **(1) same source event twice across a crash** — replaying expired source events (unchanged: duplicates; changed: one source conflict whose body goes with the expired payload) resurrects no payload and adds no root, support or claim (`test_replayed_source_after_expiry_does_not_resurrect`); the retention/backup matrix shows one expiry audit, one recorded backup per output and one deletion after kills at every new boundary. **(2) quoted or repeated through agent memory** — pins are retention only (no authority, strength, freshness or `last_supported_at` change); receipts and telemetry are never support and age out as delivery metadata. **(3) correction/forget before the final delivery fence** — what forgetting needs exists: `release_payloads` overrides pins, downgrades lost support and advances the guidance epoch in the caller's transaction (so the Task 11/12b fences exclude the old guidance; `test_forget_overrides_pin`, `test_expired_support_leaves_projections_and_packets`), every backup carries its ledger and the latest ledger copy follows the store; the tombstone writes, derivative purge and suppression-aware restore are Task 16. **(4) paths** — backup outputs never replace a path and stay out of the checkout unless under `.recalith/`; staging is beside the output and renamed in one step (Windows sharing violations retried); locations are compared resolved. **(5) provider or host version change** — retention needs no provider; the host moved to 2.1.304, the re-probe shows isolation unchanged and the route still unavailable; capture fixtures stay pinned to 2.1.301 (installed-host tests skip conservatively).
- Gaps and limits:
  - **B1** blocks every live check; production preview has no backend, so waiting model jobs keep their inputs pinned (reported as owed).
  - **Derived revision bodies** (`episode_after_closure`, `closed_task_history`) are not aged yet.
  - **Retention overrides**: the sweep honors `RetentionConfig`, but `project.toml` carries only the profile defaults today (no operator override field yet).
  - **Spool and scratch** are not part of a backup (transient intake and per-session state).
  - **Backup scale**: the backup interface copies the whole store in one step under one read transaction; the pin closure is recomputed per sweep (bounded to 4,096 candidates).
  - **Schema:** schema-8 development stores are refused, never migrated (Task 17).
  - **Crash protocol:** process death, not power loss.
- CI: **run 70 (4095098)** failed on Linux 3.12, 3.13 and 3.14 and macOS 3.13 (912 passed, 13 skipped each), in only `test_unreferenced_payloads_expire_at_180_days`, at the assertion that the day-181 sweep reported an expiry. Root cause: a test timing assumption, not retention. The sweep one second past the oldest event's 180 days already expires every unpinned payload when the fixture is captured within one second (fast CI runners); locally capture is slower, so the work split 24/6 across the two sweeps. Windows 3.13 passed in run 70 (863 passed, 63 skipped, 52:18). The fix (a22fe35, no assertion weakened) asserts that the union of both sweeps' reports equals the expired set outside the pins and that no payload is reported twice; locally `test_retention.py` passed 5 consecutive times (18 passed each) and the full suite passed 912 with 14 skipped (exit 0). **Run 71 (a22fe35): all five jobs green.** Linux 3.12 passed 913 with 13 skipped (15:27); Linux 3.13 passed 913 with 13 skipped (13:05), plus the requirements-lock check and offline provisioning; Linux 3.14 passed 913 with 13 skipped (18:25); macOS 3.13 passed 913 with 13 skipped (19:32). Windows 3.13 passed 863 with 63 skipped (52:51), running the retention and backup tests (only the POSIX permission-bits test skips; Windows relies on the profile ACLs), the guarded backup CLI subprocess and the retention/backup kill/restart matrix natively, and the runner had no orphan processes to clean up. The Windows typed-state latency gate passed; if it recurs, read the per-trial `poll_seconds` it prints.

### 2026-10-20 — Task 16 (preview): intentional forgetting and suppression-aware restore

- Files: new `src/recalith/privacy/forget.py` (selectors, the dependency closure, the purge, the persisted phases and their recovery, restore with its staging, journal and swap), `src/recalith/storage/migrations/0010.sql` (schema 10: `quarantined`/`forgotten` task and episode heads, `tombstone_targets`), tests `tests/integration/test_forgetting.py` (13), `tests/integration/test_restore.py` (7), builders `tests/integration/forget_scenarios.py`, the kill/restart matrix `test_forget_restore_crash_matches_reference` (`tests/integration/test_worker.py`, driver branch in `worker_scenarios.py`). Changed: `storage/backup.py` (`holds`, `delete_recorded`, `load_verified_snapshot`, `write_database`), `storage/db.py` (`SCHEMA_VERSION = 10`, `Store.checkpoint`), `storage/repository.py` (`suppressed` replays), `capture/pipeline.py` (suppressed counts), `controller/apply.py` (a proposal under an older tombstone epoch is stored body-less and re-evaluated in its persistence transaction; a purged application record returns nothing), `controller/worker.py` (restore recovery before the store opens, `forget.resume` first in each poll, the model lane paused during maintenance), `controller/triggers.py` and `working/reducer.py` (forgotten events are never examined or reduced; a session whose task was purged opens a new task; the reducer's refused-reduction error used an unregistered code and now raises `policy_refused`), `working/tasks.py` (counts), `core/identity.py` (`resolve_for_restore`), `fast/session.py` (the maintenance marker, `suppressed` from the latest ledger copy), `fast/delivery.py` (delivery paused: `maintenance_in_progress`, checked before compiling and under the session lock), `fast/entry.py` (no post-tool overlay for a suppressed replay or during maintenance), `fast/failpoints.py` (`failpoints.FORGET`, `failpoints.RESTORE`), `contracts/privacy.py` (`ForgetPreview.quarantined_refs`/`limits`, `RestoreResult.source_contains_suppressed`/`deleted_backup_ids`/`limits`; schemas regenerated), `service.py` (`preview_forget`, `apply_forget`, `restore`, `restore_lost_store`, the content-free `show` marker, declaration re-keying after a forget), `cli/inspect.py` and `cli/mutate.py` (`forget --preview|--apply`, `restore` applying). Tests adjusted: `test_cli.py` (forget forms now run: invalid selector input is 2), `test_retention.py` (the guarded CLI restore of a good backup now succeeds), `operation_scenarios.read_set` (the current tombstone epoch), `admission_scenarios.prompt` (forgotten payloads are `None`). Docs: decision 0017, SYSTEM_SPEC §11, DATA_CONTRACTS §3–§4, §8, §10–§12, IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN Task 16 files, coverage manifest.
- Behavior: see decision 0017. In short: `forget --selector FILE --preview` (read-only, any caller) prints the dependency-aware `ForgetPreview` (selected refs, derivatives, quarantined refs, affected backups, exports, limits, digest); `--apply --preview-digest D` (guarded) runs, under an exclusive privacy lock with delivery, the post-tool overlay and model launches paused by the maintenance marker, one purge transaction that recomputes the closure and requires the same digest (else exit 3, nothing changed), calls `retention.release_payloads` (pins overridden), purges every revision body of the closure and leaves a body-less `forgotten` or (with a surviving root) `quarantined` head, shells forgotten envelopes, drops conflict bodies, root descriptors, environment values, write paths and call pairings of forgotten events, proposal bodies, operation inputs/reasons, deliveries and outcome links naming the closure, views, lexical/FTS rows (FTS merged so deleted terms leave its shadow tables), cancels unfinished jobs over closure input, and writes the content-free tombstone, its targets, the new epoch, an audit and the persisted `privacy_operations` row; the spool and scratch are cleaned before the commit and again after it; then the latest ledger, a fresh verified backup under `.recalith/backups/forget-<id>`, deletion of affected older backups even if that backup failed (`backup_unavailable_after_forget`), reconstruction extraction jobs over surviving roots, and only then delivery resumes. Ingestion stores nothing for a tombstone target (identical or changed replay, old spool segments, transcript re-reads); a new statement has a new source identity. A late model result is fenced (cancelled job) or kept body-less and re-evaluated (older tombstone epoch). The worker resumes unfinished phases before anything else. `restore PATH --expected-digest D` verifies (Task 15), stops the worker (else 4), merges every known tombstone (the backup's, the live store's, the latest copy), suppresses the snapshot in memory, carries over backup records, privacy operations and forget/restore audits, writes the suppressed copy beside the live store, journals and atomically swaps it in after the live WAL is checkpointed, then cleans files, writes the ledger and deletes affected recorded backups; an interrupted restore is finished or discarded at the worker's start; a lost live store is restored from the checkout's own identity.
- Wired now versus later: **wired** — everything above, through the service and the guarded CLI, with recovery in the worker. **Task 17** — health composition of pending privacy maintenance (unfinished operations, `scratch_cleanup_pending`, `backup_unavailable_after_forget`, the limits) and schema migration (schema-9 stores and backups are refused). **Task 13** — certifying that the paused delivery holds on the real host. **Full release** — export/import tombstones, capsules, transfers (A38/A39/A42/A55/A71). **Still open from Task 15** — age pruning of derived revision bodies (`episode_after_closure` 180 days, `closed_task_history` 90 days): it does not fall out of the body purge (a settled episode or closed task revision cited by a live claim needs its own pin rule, and undo history depends on bodies); recorded in decision 0017.

- Verification of the tests themselves (24 deliberate mutations, each reverted; script in the session scratchpad; per mutation `test_forgetting.py` + `test_restore.py` and the matrix at 1 repetition). **Caught at once by single-process tests (18):** only the head revision's body purged, jobs over forgotten input not cancelled, replays not suppressed, spool not cleaned, scratch not cleaned, affected backups deleted before the new backup, a failed backup keeping the affected ones, restore ignoring newer tombstones, a late proposal's body stored, no quarantine (every derivative forgotten), the ledger not refreshed, the preview digest not checked, the overlay recording suppressed replays, the worker not resuming phases, deliveries not purged, the privacy operation committed in a later transaction than the purge, the model lane not paused, the marker cleared before cleanup. **Only the matrix catches** the defect *restore swapping the unsuppressed snapshot in before re-applying the tombstones* (killed at `restore.after_stage`, the live store holds forgotten text; every single-process test passes, including the at-swap delivery check). The matrix also catches the head-only purge, the spool and scratch cleanup, restore ignoring tombstones, no quarantine, the later-transaction privacy operation, the skipped FTS merge and the early marker removal. **Four first slipped past and the tests were strengthened:** skipping the FTS merge (the marker search cast blobs to text, which stops at the first NUL byte; it now scans blob bytes, including a term's prefix-compressed tail), not shelling envelopes (now asserted content-free), delivery not paused (the mutation removed only the first of two checks; with both removed it is caught), and call/result pairing kept for forgotten calls (the new `test_late_result_of_a_forgotten_call_joins_a_live_task` found a real defect: the later result of a forgotten call could not be bound — `detaches_call_result` — and the reducer's refusal raised an unregistered error code; forgetting now unpairs forgotten calls and the refusal is `policy_refused`). Synthetic states: an operator's external copy of a backup, a lost live store file, a held installation lock (a worker that does not stop), failpoint interleavings (the forget inside a hook's delivery; an `OSError` in the post-forget backup).
- Live versus unavailable (B1 still open; this session's credentials and proxy were never used as a route): with the ignored local config recreated from the tracked example (model null, `not_configured` route, unchecked attestation, request capture and live modes, no credentials, expected version the installed 2.1.304): `python tools/probe_provider.py --config probe-private/local-config.json --output <scratch>/hc.json` → exit 4 (`unavailable`; blocking `auth_route`, `live_launch`, `operator_attestation`, `schema_output`) and `python tools/check_provider_runtime.py ...` → exit 4 (clean and host-marked isolation pass; `route_uncertified`, `selected_auth_missing`), against the same Claude Code 2.1.304 executable (`ea0f1357…`). Version and verdicts unchanged, so `docs/host-compatibility.json` was not rewritten. Forgetting and restore need no model. **Unavailable:** live recall, live delivery (the nonce protocol), live review of reconstructed claims, live operator workflows on a real IDE host, every live certification.
- Review focus recheck (Task 16's part): **(1) same source event twice across a crash** — a replay of a forgotten source is suppressed at ingestion (no row, no conflict body, no root); a crash anywhere in a forget or restore converges to one tombstone, one audit, one post-forget backup and one reconstruction job (the matrix). **(2) quoted or repeated through agent memory** — receipts naming forgotten records are forgotten with them; reconstruction is ordinary extraction and review (no inherited authority); a re-typed statement is new evidence, never a revived root. **(3) correction/forget before the final delivery fence** — owned here: a forget committed before the fence withdraws the reservation, one after it is recorded `in_flight_change`; delivery stays paused until the required cleanup finished; forgotten text never reappears after restore. **(4) paths** — the post-forget backup lives under `.recalith/backups/`, staged restores beside the live store and are swapped with one atomic replace after the WAL is verified empty (Windows replace retried); envelopes lose locators; Windows behavior comes from the CI runner. **(5) provider or host version change** — forgetting needs no provider; the host is still 2.1.304, isolation unchanged, the route unavailable; fixtures stay on 2.1.301.
- Gaps and limits:
  - **B1** blocks every live check; reconstruction jobs wait (`provider_unavailable`), so quarantined claims stay unserved in production preview.
  - **Quarantined tasks and episodes** are not rebuilt (content-free; their sessions continue in new tasks).
  - **Limits of local deletion** (reported in every preview, result and restore): explicit exports, operator copies of backups and remote provider copies; filesystem forensics and a WAL a reader keeps from checkpointing; ledger digests permit membership guesses; adapters' source event keys stay as opaque identifiers (an agent-chosen `observe` key is the caller's own text); paraphrases are not detected.
  - **Scratch under contention**: a session lock held through the whole forget leaves that scratch owed (`cleanup_pending`) and delivery paused until the worker finishes it.
  - **Restore memory**: the snapshot is suppressed in memory (store-sized); spool events captured after the backup are kept and replayed through suppression; capture cursors revert with the snapshot.
  - **Derived revision bodies** are still not aged (open from Task 15).
  - **Schema:** schema-9 stores and backups are refused, never migrated (Task 17).
  - **Crash protocol:** process death, not power loss.

Root cause: the test's assumption, not delivery or the Task 16 maintenance checks. Each compile's live dependency check has a 50 ms budget (SYSTEM_SPEC §5.1); on the loaded runner one racing hook spent it, so the dependency was unknown, the caution was downgraded and its condition digest changed. An unknown dependency never matches a checked one (DATA_CONTRACTS §9), so that hook's emission is a different delivery, and its reply says `deadline_dependency_unknown`. Reproduced locally by setting one hook's budget stop time in the past: two emissions, the downgraded one 204 tokens, the same size as the extra Windows entry. The fix (no gate weakened) keeps the race's invariants: one entry, one reply and one output per emission; no record, revision and condition digest emitted twice; the lesson in every emission; consistent reservations. The checked caution may be emitted by at most one hook, and any further emission must report `deadline_dependency_unknown`. New deterministic test `test_unknown_dependency_caution_is_a_distinct_delivery` (A59): spent budget → emitted and degraded; checked → emitted once; a repeat of either → suppressed. Disabling suppression still fails the race test (6 identical keys). Locally the two tests passed 3 consecutive times, and the full suite passed 945 with 14 skipped in 1489.42 s (Python 3.13.16, exit 0). **Run 76 (3a9326c): all five jobs green.** Linux 3.12 passed 946 with 13 skipped (20:41); Linux 3.13 passed 946 with 13 skipped (19:19), plus the requirements-lock check and offline provisioning; Linux 3.14 passed 946 with 13 skipped (17:58); macOS 3.13 passed 946 with 13 skipped (18:39). - Gaps and limits:
  - **B1** blocks every live check; a real installation cannot be ready until Task 13 writes `host.json` and an operator certifies a route.
  - **Static route check without a worker** judges the selected key's presence in the caller's environment, not the future worker's; the running worker's own report is used when available.
  - **Read bookkeeping:** a read-only snapshot of an idle WAL store may create an empty `-wal` and the `-shm` index (SQLite's own; no frame, no database byte).
  - **Retention cost:** health recomputes the live pin closure on each call (O(store)).
  - **Thresholds** (heartbeat 300 s, ingestion delay 10 min) are engineering defaults, not measured gates.
  - **Not built (full-only):** upgrade/transition/rollback, OS startup entries, chain diagnostics; still open from Task 15: age pruning of derived revision bodies.

Request capture showed all four paths `request_visible`, command rules `denied_all_covered` and private paths `probed_limited`; live was `not_run` (`no_auth_route_configured`); the target's `host.json` is `mode: request_capture` with every delivery path `unavailable`.

- **Review focus recheck** (Task 13's part):
  1. **The same source event twice across a crash.** Installed hooks capture through the unchanged spool and dedup paths. The integration's own persisted steps converge under the kill/restart matrix, with no duplicate owned entry. A restarted hook-spawned worker drains the backlog once.
  2. **Quoted or repeated through agent memory.** Delivery receipts and nonce trials add no support. The certification harness's memories are recorded fixtures. A request-capture observation never certifies a path, and a delivery is not evidence.
  3. **A correction or forget before the final delivery fence.** The installed hooks run the unchanged Task 12b/16 fence and maintenance pause. The notice names a paused delivery, and health keeps `maintenance_in_progress`.
  4. **Paths.** The hook command is project-relative. A move keeps the hook off until the operator rebinds and integration re-runs. A clone carries no settings. A copied or foreign `cwd` still captures nothing. Paths with spaces and Unicode work. The private-path probe never installs rules in the project.
  5. **A provider or host version change.** Delivery paths are enabled only for the exact certified version. A version change after integration reports `host_version_changed` in health. An uncertified version is conservative: capture only, no delivery, no notice. 2.1.305 was certified only after its fixture review; fixtures were not re-pinned.
- **Gaps and limits:**
  - **B1** blocks live certification. No real project has delivery enabled.
  - **Platforms:** the hook command is POSIX. Native Windows and macOS host execution and their termination behavior were not observed; the deterministic installer tests run on every OS in CI.
  - **Session start:** with no task binding, a fresh session start carries only open intent questions (decision 0013), so the start trials use a question.
  - **Deny rules** are friction, not a boundary: global options, shell wrappers and other interpreters evade them, and the CLI guard is the backstop.
  - **Private-path protection** on 2.1.305 is limited (`sh -c`, interpreters); preview installs none.
  - **The notice** is shown to the user by the host; that it appears in an interactive terminal is inferred from the host's `systemMessage` contract and its stream event, not observed in a TTY here.
  - **Still open from Task 15:** age pruning of derived revision bodies.

  - Linux 3.13: 1042 passed, 27 skipped (tests 1326.33 s), plus the requirements-lock check and offline provisioning (1 passed).
  - Linux 3.14: 1042 passed, 27 skipped (tests 1318.17 s).
  - macOS 3.13: 1042 passed, 27 skipped (tests 1469.16 s).

  - Windows 3.13: 1 failed, 975 passed, 93 skipped (tests 3116.46 s). `test_deny_rules_cover_admin_forms_in_launcher_and_module_spellings` built its expected absolute launcher spelling with `str(root)` (backslashes on Windows), while the installer deliberately writes the forward-slash `root.as_posix()` form on every OS. The test was fixed, not the code: `PureWindowsPath(...)` shows the two forms differ only on Windows. The POSIX-only installed-command tests skip there by design. The installer, removal, guard, health, matrix and version-review tests ran natively.
- **CI run 85 (17f5ea8): Windows failed the same test again; this time the cause was a real defect.**
  - **Failure:** Windows 3.13: 1 failed, 975 passed, 93 skipped (tests 3125.92 s). The other jobs passed.
  - **Root cause:** the CLI wrote its `--json` stdout and its stderr diagnostics in the stream's text encoding. A Windows pipe defaults to the console code page, so the `é` of the test project's path left the child as `0xE9` and read back as U+FFFD. A character outside the code page raises `UnicodeEncodeError`.
  - **Reproduced locally** with `TextIOWrapper` streams in `cp1252` and `ascii`: `test_cli.py::test_machine_output_is_utf8_whatever_the_console_encoding` failed on both before the fix.
  - **Fix:** `cli/main.py` writes UTF-8 bytes to the stream's buffer for both streams. The forward-slash spelling fix in the deny-rule test stays: it was needed too.
  - **Checks after the fix:** the unit, CLI, adapter and health suites → 504 passed in 462.91 s.
- **CI run 86 (d436a1c): all five jobs green.** The 3 extra tests per job are the parametrized UTF-8 regression test.
  - Linux 3.12: 1045 passed, 27 skipped (tests 1366.21 s).
  - Linux 3.13: 1045 passed, 27 skipped (tests 1282.30 s), plus the requirements-lock check and offline provisioning (1 passed, 3.93 s).
  - Linux 3.14: 1045 passed, 27 skipped (tests 1146.38 s).
  - macOS 3.13: 1045 passed, 27 skipped (tests 1611.80 s).
  - Windows 3.13: 979 passed, 93 skipped (tests 3125.07 s).

Every pre-action caution is delivered, and every prompt-specific fact is missing, because the hook's views hold a query-independent slice. Prompt trials are therefore all degraded, and delivery is about 50 % against targets of ≥ 99 % warm and ≥ 95 % cold. A Task 11 view-contract change is needed. Run 2 kept no series, so its tail cannot be attributed: it is not established as a code cost, and it is not dismissed. The cold-cache latency target is therefore **not established on Windows** until repeated runs agree; shared CI runners are not the §7 reference hardware.

- **Review focus recheck** (Task 18's part):
  1. **The same source event twice across a crash.** The 20-rep matrix converges on the reference in all 13 families. The aging step is idempotent: a re-run after `retention.after_record_aging` ages nothing twice and writes one audit. Each trial's store is its own, and the evidence horizon refuses foreign or future events.
  2. **Quoted or repeated through agent memory.** Delivery receipts are excluded from evidence judging, and the recorded curator's proposals pass through the unchanged admission and lineage. A demonstration or workload replay adds no support root beyond its own recorded events.
  3. **A correction or forget before the final delivery fence.** R3's correction suppresses the old budget in the next packet. The forget demonstration's fence and maintenance pause hold, and the restore applies the newer tombstone before serving. Aged bodies advance the guidance epoch, so stale views are refused.
  4. **Paths.** Trials, days and demonstrations run in disposable owned workspaces through the unchanged registered-boundary resolution (the Task 13 suites cover spaces, Unicode and relocation). Displayed commands replace the home prefix, and the checked-in reports carry no absolute paths.
  5. **A provider or host version change.** 2.1.306 was captured, reviewed and only then certified. The native-memory observation is re-run per run and labels contamination. Preflight rereads the host record per run, and a request-capture record never certifies.

  - **Root causes and fixes:**
    - **The race test failure is a real cost of this slice's aging step.** It opened a write transaction on every sweep, including every worker poll, and computed the full pin closure under the lock even when nothing could age. The sweep now takes candidates from its read snapshot and locks only when a body can age. `test_sweep_takes_no_aging_transaction_when_nothing_can_age` fails on the old sweep and passes now.
    - **The backup test's writer thread re-took the lock back to back** and starved the backup's short record write. It now pauses 5 ms between writes, so writes still land throughout the backup.
    - **The two delivery tests judge accounting and kill outcomes**, yet they ran with the production 250 ms deadline on a shared runner. They now run the real `entry.main` in a fresh `python -I` process through a test-only driver with a generous deadline. Deadline shedding keeps its own tests and is measured per platform; production gains no switch.

- **Design** (decision 0021): a per-workspace lookup projection the hook reads with the standard library inside the existing deadline.
  - **Worker side.** Each live claim head gets one pre-rendered candidate row in the view primitive form. A null body records that the claim is not servable in that workspace. Bound tasks are stored scope-only and re-read live.
  - **Postings.** Each term of a servable row gets one posting. The terms are the candidate's own `terms` from `fast.context.terms`, so the indexer and the hook use one tokenizer.
  - **Reconciliation.** The worker reconciles when the epochs differ from its recorded ones:
    - it renders at most 512 owed claims in a read snapshot, outside the write lock;
    - it writes them in one short transaction that re-reads the epochs and discards the batch if they moved;
    - it deletes at most 4,096 stale rows per transaction;
    - it records the epochs only when nothing is owed.
  - **Volatile rows** (disputed, non-current, claim dependencies, unservable) are re-rendered at each guidance epoch.
  - **Hook caps.** The lookup runs only for `prompt`, warm or cold, and only with ≥ 60 ms left (else `deadline_lookup_skipped`). It reads:
    - bounded df counts (≤ 1,025) for the first 32 distinct query terms;
    - ≤ 64 strongest postings for each of the 8 rarest present terms;
    - ≤ 16 rows, by summed weight `1 + ln(1025/df)`, skipping IDs the view or fallback already holds.
  - **Row filters.** Every fallback filter applies, plus:
    - the policy revision equals the store's (else `lookup_policy_changed`);
    - no tombstone (`tombstone_targets`) newer than the row names the claim or a cited ID;
    - the head is at the row's revision, status and freshness, and is live;
    - some support event still holds available or capsule-only evidence;
    - quoted events are still in their rendered payload state;
    - the environment digest matches;
    - each version condition with a known value carries the exact precomputed result.
    Survivors go through `fast.context.decide`, the budget and the fresh final fence.
  - **Isolation and forgetting.** Rows are keyed by workspace, and the hook opens only its own store. The forget purge deletes, in its own transaction, every row of a purged record and every row citing one. Restore empties both tables.
  - **Invariants kept:**
    - no SQLite write, lock wait, model or network on the hook path;
    - `recalith.fast` stays stdlib-only, and `fast/lookup.py` does not even import `sqlite3`.
- **Commands** (Linux x86_64 container, 4 vCPU Intel Xeon @ 2.10 GHz, SQLite 3.45.1, Python 3.13.16, Claude Code 2.1.306; `git status` clean at `7c3268d` before the change):

  - **Run 8's per-trial series gives a partial attribution:**
    - The two late trials (6.54 and 6.60 s) had fast worker polls (slowest ≤ 0.06 s), so they missed poll cycles rather than waiting on worker work.
    - On Windows, arrival phase plus latency comes to about 6–6.5 s even for late-phase arrivals, against about 5 s on Linux and macOS. The worker's observed idle cycle there runs 1–1.5 s past its 5 s cadence.
    - Separately, some polls took 1.3–2.8 s (trials 6–7 and 15–16) while every Linux and macOS poll stayed ≤ 0.07 s.
  - **Cause not established.** The lookup adds 0–5 ms to a projection step (median 19.9 vs 20.1 ms at 20 heads and 58.7 vs 53.6 ms at 2,000, old vs new code, in this container), which cannot explain seconds. A same-runner comparison with the previous commit needs a ref to dispatch from, which this session may not push. This is an **open §7 item on Windows**, recorded with the attribution above; the target is not weakened.
- **Idle worker, CI** (600 s; targets < 1 % of one core, ≤ 150 MiB): every run meets it.
  - Linux: 0.075–0.097 %, 46.5–47.3 MiB.
  - macOS: 0.20–0.22 %, 40.3–44.8 MiB.
  - Windows: 0.138–0.159 %, 15.5–16.1 MiB.
- **Checklist:** `hook_latency_usefulness_per_platform` is `failed`.
  - Every hook latency and usefulness target is met on every native platform, and the idle footprint holds.
  - It is not `passed`, because the Windows typed-state p95 target is unmet in three of four runs.
  - `tools/release_checklist.py` → exit 0 (verdict `blocked`).
- **Next action:** attribute the Windows typed-state latency (the per-trial series now in the worker report), then the live preview gate once B1 clears, then Cut 2.

### 2026-10-22 — Windows typed-state latency (open §7 item): attribution, measurement fix and deferred WAL checkpoint (decision 0022; commits 0b0af8a, 8caa1cb, 36bbc06, 2190a38, b3f1f1f)

- **Starting point.**
  - Branch `ccr-b49de118-0mxrev` (3a41f40, 8c57a62, 4880090) is merged into main (PR #19, 6d60c10). This work builds on 6d60c10, and the tree was clean.
  - Baseline: `.venv/bin/python -m pytest -q -rs -p no:cacheprovider` → exit 0, **1084 passed, 28 skipped in 1783.28 s**. The 8 extra skips against the last checkpoint are the host smoke tests: the installed CLI is now **Claude Code 2.1.307**, which is not capture-certified and was not certified here.
  - A first baseline attempt was stopped by PID after `worker.py` was edited while it ran, then restarted on the clean tree.
- **Attribution** (series in `docs/measurements/ci-typed-state-2026-10-22/`, indexed in its README):
  - Instrumentation was added first, measurement-only:
    - the worker status gained `poll.timing`;
    - the tool gained an outside observer (segment visibility, event row, ingest completion, each status write, store-file writes, its own stalls);
    - `measure.yml` gained dispatch inputs `runners`, `worker_only`, `trials` and `idle_seconds`.
  - Windows worker-only dispatches: measure runs 11, 13, 14, 16, 17, 19, 20, 22 and 23 (40–60 trials each). Full runs: 10, 21 and 24.
  - **The worker's cadence on Windows is exact.** Every sleep matched its request within 10–15 ms, and every segment was ingested by the first poll that started after it appeared. There is no drift and no missed segment.
  - **Run 8's "idle cycle 1–1.5 s past the cadence" was the tool.**
    - It planned each arrival from the idle status written *before* the previous trial's ingesting poll, because that poll had not yet replaced it.
    - Run 11: 25 of 40 plans were stale on Windows; Linux CI had 2 of 20.
    - The phases were recorded against a cycle that had already ended. This did not raise latencies, because a stale plan sometimes put the arrival in the 1 s active window.
  - **"Fast polls" was a one-poll lag.** `last_poll_seconds` was set after the status write, so run 8's late trials 5 and 14 (6.54 and 6.60 s) carried their own ingesting polls' 2.82 and 2.33 s into trials 6 and 15.
  - **The real outliers are slow ingesting polls during runner storage stalls.**
    - These are polls of 1.8–5 s, against a typical 0.05–0.15 s, split between `ingest_ready`'s commit and the ingest job's commits.
    - The tool's own file-only loop stalled 0.25–0.73 s in the same windows.
    - Linux and macOS polls stayed ≤ 0.1 s.
  - **SQLite's auto-checkpoint ran inside the ingesting poll's commits.**
    - `ingest_ready` opened a fresh connection with SQLite's defaults.
    - Locally the main file grew from 0.75 to 5.7 MB before the ingest job committed.
    - On the runners it was usually quick and never fell in a late trial. It was the heaviest avoidable I/O on the path, not the main cause.
- **Changes** (decision 0022):
  - `tools/measure_worker_latency.py`:
    - `idle_status(root, after=...)` plans each arrival from an idle poll that started after the previous trial reached typed state;
    - the two-thread observer and per-trial attribution;
    - recovery of a missed poll from the next status.
  - `tests/platform/test_worker_lifecycle.py`: the same fresh-settle rule.
  - `src/recalith/controller/worker.py`:
    - `poll.timing` (step marks, the sleep before the poll, the previous status write, the previous poll);
    - `last_poll_seconds` describes the poll the status follows;
    - counters `checkpoints` and `status_write_failures`;
    - the worker's connection defers auto-checkpoints, and a PASSIVE checkpoint runs at most once per 60 s in a poll with nothing waiting (after a poll's work once 120 s have passed under continuous work).
  - The first deferral (2190a38) checkpointed after every burst. Windows runs 19 and 20 showed 61 checkpoints per 60 trials and a 7.6 s one stretching an idle cycle, so b3f1f1f restored SQLite's frequency.
  - `src/recalith/storage/db.py`: `Store.defer_autocheckpoint`, `Store.checkpoint_passive`.
  - `src/recalith/capture/pipeline.py`: `ingest_ready(..., store=)`.
  - `src/recalith/fast/failpoints.py`: `worker.before_checkpoint`, `worker.after_checkpoint` (`failpoints.CHECKPOINT`).
  - `.github/workflows/measure.yml`: the dispatch inputs (a push still runs the full default set everywhere).
  - No schema, DTO, wire or spec change. The worker status is not a DATA_CONTRACTS record, and the change is recorded in decision 0022.

### 2026-10-22 — Direct-API inference backends (decision 0023; Task 7 follow-up)

- **Scope.** An operator can now certify a direct API as the background curator's route instead of only the Claude CLI: `anthropic_messages` (Messages dialect) and `openai_compatible` (Chat Completions dialect: hosted providers and loopback local servers). Not started, by instruction: the live preview gate (B1 still needs the operator), subscription harnesses (Codex exec, Copilot SDK, Cursor and similar; Codex inference stays Task 14), Cut 2/full-only work, upgrades/transition, transfers and capsules. The requested record number 0022 was already taken by the Windows typed-state decision, so this is decision **0023**.
- **Files.**
  - New: `src/recalith/providers/api.py` (dialects: exact request bodies, header names, transport specification, strict reply reading, adapter digest), `src/recalith/providers/http_transport.py` (stdlib-only transport child, launched `python -I <path>`), `tools/probe_api.py`, `tools/probe_config.{anthropic_messages,openai_compatible,local_server}.example.json`, `tests/fixtures/providers/fake_api_server.py` (loopback fake provider), `tests/unit/test_api_providers.py`, `tests/unit/test_api_probe.py`, `tests/integration/test_api_worker.py`, `docs/decisions/0023-direct-api-inference-backends.md`.
  - Changed: `contracts/config.py` (`ApiEndpoint`; `ProviderConfig.backend` gains two kinds, `executable` optional, `invocation_args` defaulted, `endpoint`, `expected_resolved_model`; `AuthRouteKind` `none`; `ProviderCertification.resolved_model`/`failed_checks`, attestation `not_applicable_local`; backend-specific completeness moved to `ProviderConfig`), schemas regenerated (`ProviderConfig.schema.json`, `index.json`; 47 snapshots), `providers/base.py` (adapters, API invocation digest, route status with `adapter_changed` and `certification_failed:<check>`, per-call output cap, transport stdin, `_spawned` hook, `last_failure`, `last_call_failure` in the supervisor report, `ProviderCallFailed` usage charged), `providers/errors.py`, `providers/isolation.py` (`CaptureEndpoint`, `api_isolation_check`, `capture_launch`, `episode_body`), `operations/health.py` (a certified route's last call failure is a degraded inference reason), `integrations/claude.py` (the hook-started worker carries the route's network key names too), `tools/probe_provider.py` (`--backend`, API configuration), `tools/check_provider_runtime.py` (`--backend`, API branch), `tools/probe_config.example.json` (pointer to the API examples), `evals/run.py` (generalized operator steps), `tests/integration/provider_scenarios.py` and `tests/integration/test_worker.py` (API crash variant), `tests/platform/test_hook_imports.py` (hook forbids `recalith.providers`; transport child audit), `tests/coverage_manifest.json`, `docs/release/claude_preview_checklist.json`, SYSTEM_SPEC §9.1, DATA_CONTRACTS §11, IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN Task 7, SOURCES_AND_REUSE §4, `docs/probes/LOCAL_CONFIG.md`, `docs/OPERATIONS.md`, `docs/host-compatibility.json` (three new request-capture sections).
- **Design (decision 0023).** Each call runs `python -I src/recalith/providers/http_transport.py` (stdlib only, never `recalith`) through `run_bounded` with the curator environment and private root; the parent builds the whole request and the child adds the key from its own environment by name, sends once and writes a header line plus the raw body. TLS is always verified (`ssl.create_default_context`; a managed bundle only through `SSL_CERT_FILE`/`SSL_CERT_DIR` named in `network_env_keys`); `http://` only to `127.0.0.1`, `::1`, `localhost` (validator and child); proxies only from named keys (`getproxies_environment`, never system settings); no redirect handler; no retry. Request bodies: Messages `{model, max_tokens, system, messages}` (no native JSON mode: `output_config.format` adds a hidden system prompt and rejects the engine's schema constraints); Chat Completions `{model, messages, <cap field>, response_format json_object}` (strict `json_schema` not used; `json_mode: none` and `output_cap_field: max_tokens` exist for servers that need them). The output cap is the purpose allowance (1,500/8,000/8,000/3,000/3,000 tokens). Replies: a reported model other than `expected_resolved_model or configured_model`, a tool call, a reported output above the cap, 401/403/404 or a 3xx withdraws the route; a cap stop, a refusal, 4xx/5xx/429/529, unreachable endpoints, TLS failures and truncated bodies are charged failures with normal backoff; usage maps input (including cache) and output counts, unknown usage charges the engine's estimates.
- **Certification.** Request capture (`providers.isolation.api_isolation_check`): one POST to a loopback capture endpoint with the configured path and query; exact header-name set; the placeholder only in the configured auth header; no other credential-shaped inherited value anywhere; body byte-identical to the engine's with exactly the dialect's keys; model, cap and JSON mode; the production parser reads the reply; the observed child environment holds only constructed keys and the curator marker. The worker's supervisor runs it from its own environment before launching (decision 0009 item 4). Live certification (`tools/probe_provider.py --backend …`, `tools/probe_api.py`): clean and host-marked parents; auth, reported model, a schema-valid no-op within the timeout, a 32-token cap call that must stop at the cap, the endpoint origin (verified TLS unless loopback, no redirect), usage recorded; attestation `checked_eligible` (or `not_applicable_local` for loopback). Only that run writes `certified: true`; an uncertified record lists `failed_checks`, reported as `certification_failed:<check>`. Invocation digest: backend, provider, models, the whole endpoint, key names, switches, network keys; `executable_sha256` pins the adapter sources (`adapter_changed` on change).
- **Tests first.** The new tests were written before the implementation and failed: `tests/unit/test_api_providers.py` and `tests/integration/test_api_worker.py` at collection (`cannot import name 'api'`), and 13 failures across `test_api_probe.py`, the transport import audit and the API crash matrix (`ProviderConfig` rejected `openai_compatible` and `auth_route: none`).
- **Commands and results (Linux x86_64 container, SQLite 3.45.1):**
  - Baseline before any change (7f8386a, 3.13): `.venv/bin/python -m pytest -q -rs -p no:cacheprovider` → exit 1, 1090 passed, 1 failed, 28 skipped in 2192 s. The failure (`test_timeout_terminates_process_tree`: a curator root appeared in the shared temp directory) was cross-run interference: test runs in a scratch copy were creating curator roots in the same `/tmp` at the time. It passed alone on the clean tree (1 passed in 3.4 s). A first baseline attempt was stopped because tests/ had been edited while it ran; the work moved to a scratch copy with its own virtualenv and was ported back with `cmp`.
  - Full suite on the slice (783e876 + a696ca7): 3.13 → exit 0, **1193 passed, 28 skipped in 2700 s**; `UV_PROJECT_ENVIRONMENT=<scratch> uv run --locked --python 3.12 python -m pytest -q -rs -p no:cacheprovider` → exit 0, **1193 passed, 28 skipped in 2550 s**.
  - After the Windows fix (6afb75b): 3.14 → exit 0, **1194 passed, 28 skipped in 2236 s**; the provider suites on 3.13 (`tests/unit/test_api_providers.py tests/unit/test_provider_contract.py tests/platform/test_provider_isolation.py tests/unit/test_api_probe.py`) → 110 passed in 87 s.
  - `RECALITH_CRASH_REPS=20 .venv/bin/python -m pytest -q -rs -p no:cacheprovider tests/integration/test_worker.py -k provider_path_crash` → exit 0, **18 passed in 2305 s**: the JSON-process provider-path family and the new direct-API family, 9 failpoints each (`failpoints.WORKER` + `failpoints.PROVIDER`), 20 kill/restart repetitions each.
  - `tools/generate_schemas.py --check` and `tools/generate_hook_wire.py --check` → exit 0 (schemas regenerated: `ProviderConfig`, `index`). `uvx ruff@0.14.0 check --select F,E9,B` on every touched Python file → clean. `tools/build_release.py --out <scratch>` → exit 0, `problems: []`, the wheel contains `providers/api.py` and `providers/http_transport.py`; no dependency added (`DEPENDENCIES.json`, locks unchanged). `tools/release_checklist.py` → exit 0, verdict `blocked`, with `inference_isolation_and_delivery_certified` still `blocked` (B1: no live-certified route of any backend).
  - Request-capture probes with ignored configs recreated from the three new examples (`modes: ["request_capture"]`; no key, no network): `tools/probe_provider.py --backend anthropic_messages|openai_compatible` → exit 4 each (`request_shape`, `environment_isolation`, `bounds_and_cancellation` pass; live dimensions `not_run`), 14 s each; `tools/check_provider_runtime.py --backend …` → exit 4 each (clean and host-marked request capture pass; `route_uncertified`, plus `selected_auth_missing` where a key is named), 2 s each. Recorded in `docs/host-compatibility.json` (`anthropic_api`, `openai_compatible_api`, `local_model`; the existing sections are unchanged). The Claude CLI probes were not re-run.
- **Verification of the tests themselves** (deliberate mutations in the scratch copy, each reverted; script in the session scratchpad): `reaper_keeps_spawn_records` and `interrupted_reservations_not_charged` passed every non-matrix suite (unit, API worker, requests, provider isolation, worker lifecycle: 396 passed, 2 skipped) and were caught only by the API crash matrix (1 and 2 failures). `api_spawn_unrecorded` (no spawn record for API children) first slipped past everything: the transport child exits as soon as its dead parent's pipe closes, so survival could not show it. The matrix now asserts the spawn record exists when the worker dies at `provider.after_spawn`, and the mutation fails it.
- **Scripted evaluation:** `python evals/run.py gate --mode scripted --init-route test_fixture --out <scratch>/eval-gate` → exit 8 (`blocked`: B1, not live) in 107 s. Compared with the same command on the previous commit (7f8386a, a temporary worktree, same output-path length) → exit 8 in 111 s: every smoke outcome, token count and demonstration is identical; the differences are the order of two equally ranked admitted claims and the fieldnotes day making 36 calls instead of 37, the pre-existing variance recorded in the Task 11 follow-up. Against `docs/evaluations/preview-recorded-2026-10-21/` the foreground/background token counts differ by a few tokens in both arms on both commits (environment paths), and the schema and host version differ. `docs/evaluations/` is not updated.
- **Idle and hook paths:** unchanged (no `fast/` module changed; the worker's poll loop is unchanged), so neither `tools/measure_worker_latency.py` nor `measure.yml` was re-run. The hook import audit now forbids `recalith.providers` explicitly, and a fresh-process audit shows the transport child imports only the standard library and makes one connection.
- **CI:** run 113 (a696ca7): Linux 3.12 1194 passed, 27 skipped in 1730 s; Linux 3.13 1194 passed, 27 skipped in 1559 s (and the provisioning step); Linux 3.14 1194 passed, 27 skipped in 1675 s; macOS 3.13 1194 passed, 27 skipped in 1922 s; **Windows 3.13 failed: 1 failed, 1127 passed, 93 skipped in 4337 s**. The failure was a real defect (`test_proxy_settings_reach_the_child_only_when_named`): Windows stores environment names upper-cased, so a route naming `http_proxy` was reported missing and withdrawn as `selected_auth_missing`. ### 2026-10-22 — Task 5 full slice: the revision hash chain and integrity scanner (decision 0024; Cut 2; commits 6ec7ab8, d1b05c7)

- **Why this task.** B1 is still open: every route in `docs/host-compatibility.json` is `certified: false` with live `not_run`, there is no live `.recalith/host.json`, and the owner's instruction carried no note clearing it. So the live preview gate cannot run, and per instruction Cut 2 starts with Task 5's full slice. Not started, by instruction: the live gate, the subscription-auth question, Task 14, upgrades/transition, transfers and capsules, session mode/IPC wake and every other Cut 2 slice. The starting point (1c9f337) contains decision 0022 (through 3ece9a4) and decision 0023; the tree was clean. The next free decision number was 0024.
- **Files.**
  - New: `src/recalith/storage/integrity.py`, `docs/decisions/0024-revision-chain-and-integrity-scanner.md`.
  - Changed: `storage/db.py` (`Store.transaction` arms the capture and seals the entry before `COMMIT` in a full store; `create_database` writes the init baseline; `Txn.purged()`), `core/identity.py` (the operator rebind's raw transaction is chained), `fast/failpoints.py` (`CHAIN` family: `chain.before_seal`, `chain.after_seal`), `privacy/forget.py` and `privacy/retention.py` (purge markers), `operations/health.py` (full-profile `revision_chain` capability; preview unchanged), `service.py` and `cli/inspect.py` (`doctor` verifies first; `--full-integrity`; findings exit 8), `controller/apply.py` (docstring), `tests/integration/test_integrity.py` (the five skips replaced by 17 full-profile tests plus the preview test), `tests/integration/test_worker.py` (`test_full_profile_chain_crash_matches_reference`), `tests/integration/worker_scenarios.py` (count mode logs `CHAIN`), `tests/platform/claude_fixtures.py` (test-only `RELEASE_PROFILE`), `tests/coverage_manifest.json` (A65, A21), DATA_CONTRACTS §11/§12, SYSTEM_SPEC §10, IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN Task 5, OPERATIONS §3.
- **Design (decision 0024).** Only a `release_profile: full` store records or verifies a chain; `init --release-profile full` stays refused, so full stores exist only through the provisioning function with a full `ProjectConfig` (tests). Every write transaction that changes a chained row (every ordinary table except `revision_chain`; FTS5 shadow tables are not ordinary tables) appends one entry inside that transaction just before `COMMIT`; per-connection TEMP triggers capture touched rows (rollbacks and savepoint rollbacks discard them), so no writer registers rows and none can forget to. Entries are digests only: canonical `row_digests` with sorted `[table, key_digest, rowid, row_digest|null]` items, the `installation_meta` row digest as metadata hash, the operation IDs; `entry_hash` over `[sequence, previous_hash, operation_id, row_digests, purge_marker, tombstone_epoch]` from 64 zeros. A new full store starts with an `init` baseline; `establish_baseline(txn, reason="activation")` is the upgrade's call for a preview store and reports earlier history `not_chained`. Forget (including restore's suppression), retention expiry, derived-body aging, bookkeeping aging and the forgetting override append `purge` entries and never rewrite history. Verification: default `doctor` re-links the entries after the trusted checkpoint and compares the rows they touched; `--full-integrity` re-links the whole chain and compares every current row by key digest (O(store), progress on stderr). The checkpoint is the private file `.recalith/state/integrity.json` (0600), never the store, so `doctor` keeps the store read-only; findings never advance it and add `revision_chain_findings` (exit 8). Health reports the chain in full stores from the head, baseline and checkpoint without verifying; it never gates readiness. Preview never writes the chain and reports every form `unavailable_in_preview`.
- **Contract changes.** No schema migration: `SCHEMA_VERSION` stays 11 and the reserved columns of migration 0001 are used as they are; the entry format, `doctor`'s full-profile `checks.revision_chain` object, the `revision_chain_findings` degraded code and the checkpoint file are new contract text (DATA_CONTRACTS §11/§12). The preview test `test_preview_revision_chain_is_reserved_and_unpopulated` no longer asserts that `recalith.storage.integrity` cannot be imported (the module now exists); it asserts instead that every integrity form on a preview store is `capability_unavailable` (`unavailable_in_preview`), that the activation baseline is refused there, that no checkpoint file is written and that `revision_chain` stays empty.
- **Tests first.** The new tests were written before the implementation and failed at collection: `cannot import name 'integrity' from 'recalith.storage'` (test_integrity.py) and `module 'recalith.fast.failpoints' has no attribute 'CHAIN'` (test_worker.py). While implementing, three test expectations were corrected against real behavior, none weakening a check: a new store also holds the singleton bookkeeping rows its migrations seed (the baseline covers exactly the current rows); restore's post-swap ledger/backup-policy transaction is an ordinary entry after the staged purge entry; the forged-tail case now forges a self-consistent entry (an inconsistent one is correctly `entry_malformed`). A deliberate mutation (sealing in a separate transaction after `COMMIT`) failed both chain failpoints of the new crash matrix with `row_digest_mismatch`, then was reverted.
- **Commands and results (Linux x86_64 container, SQLite 3.45.1, 4 cores; development in a separate git worktree with its own virtualenv and TMPDIR, fast-forwarded into this tree after the baseline):**
  - Baseline before any change (1c9f337, 3.13): `.venv/bin/python -m pytest -q -rs -p no:cacheprovider` → exit 0, **1194 passed, 28 skipped in 1950 s**.
  - Full suite on the slice (d1b05c7): 3.13 → exit 0, **1220 passed, 23 skipped in 2170 s** (26 more passing and 5 fewer skipped: the five skipped A65 placeholders became 17 full-profile tests, plus the 9 cases of the new kill/restart matrix); `TMPDIR=<scratch> UV_PROJECT_ENVIRONMENT=<scratch> uv run --locked --python 3.12 python -m pytest -q -rs -p no:cacheprovider` → exit 0, **1220 passed, 23 skipped in 2138 s**; the same with `--python 3.14` → exit 0, **1220 passed, 23 skipped in 2075 s** (3.12 and 3.14 in parallel, each with its own TMPDIR).
  - Kill/restart at `RECALITH_CRASH_REPS=20` (each with its own TMPDIR): the new full-profile chain family (`-k chain_crash`, `failpoints.CHAIN` + `failpoints.WORKER`, chain kills spread over every chained transaction a counting run hits) → exit 0, **9 passed in 374 s**; the forget/restore family (`-k forget_restore_crash`; `forget.py` changed) → exit 0, **12 passed in 541 s**; the retention/backup family (`-k retention_backup_crash`; `retention.py` changed) → exit 0, **9 passed in 537 s**; the preview worker-boundary family (`-k worker_crash_at_job_boundaries`; `Store.transaction` changed) → exit 0, **7 passed in 224 s**. The other families ran at the default 3 repetitions in the full suites (the preview change to their path is one profile comparison).
  - Idle polls append nothing: a full-profile worker after a drain made 0 entries over 10 idle polls.
  - `uvx ruff@0.14.0 check --select F,E9,B` on every touched Python file → clean. `tools/generate_schemas.py --check` and `tools/generate_hook_wire.py --check` → exit 0 (no contract model changed). `tools/release_checklist.py` → exit 0, verdict `blocked`, `problems: []`; `inference_isolation_and_delivery_certified`, `smoke_quality_gates`, `deterministic_case_variants` and `release_artifacts` still `blocked` (B1 and owner decisions), unchanged.

The tool left its `/tmp/recalith-worker-latency-*` directory (pre-existing behavior); it was removed by path after checking no process used it. The push also ran `measure.yml` (run 26, d1b05c7), green on all three runners: typed-state p95 from the hook spawn Windows 4.53 s, Linux 4.50 s, macOS 4.52 s (n=20 each); idle CPU 0.18 / 0.085 / 0.24 % of one core, RSS 16.2 / 46.9 / 39.3 MiB; the warm and cold-missing hook gates met (warm entry-to-exit p95 61.7 / 29.2 / 25.4 ms). Decision 0022's picture is unchanged.

- **Acceptance** (none passed; live variants still blocked by B1): **A65** full variant `pending` → `in_progress` (chain and row verification built and tested; Task 13's installed private-path rules remain); A65 preview variant unchanged in status, its note records that preview still reports the chain unavailable; **A21** gains the full-profile chain kill/restart matrix. `tests/coverage_manifest.json` lists the tests.
- **Gaps and limits:**

### 2026-10-23 — Task 15 full capsule slice: exact evidence capsules, link redirection and retained pins (decision 0025; Cut 2; commits 870af5c, a400490, 599e11d)

- **Why this task.** B1 is still open: every route in `docs/host-compatibility.json` is `certified: false` with live `not_run`, there is no live `.recalith/host.json`, and the owner's instruction carried no note clearing it. So the live preview gate cannot run, and per instruction Cut 2 continues with Task 15's full capsule slice, which the Task 17 preview-to-full transition depends on. Not started, by instruction: the live gate, the subscription-auth question, Task 14, upgrades and the transition (Task 17 full), transfers (export, import, the Breadcrumbs importer), session mode/IPC wake and every other Cut 2 slice. The starting point (a353d83) contains decision 0024 (6ec7ab8, d1b05c7, c7214b9 and CI run 119); the tree was clean; `.venv` was recreated with `uv sync --locked --python 3.13`. The next free decision number was 0025.
- **Files.**
  - New: `src/recalith/privacy/capsules.py`, `tests/integration/test_capsules.py` (12 tests), `docs/decisions/0025-exact-evidence-capsules.md`.
  - Changed: `privacy/retention.py` (the compaction step after expiry, the pin closure split into live claims, working context, payload-owing and claim-input work; capsule-only expiry; `release_payloads` reaches capsules; report fields), `core/lineage.py` (capsule refs walk to their origin root; `chain_available` accepts covered capsule-only evidence; `claims_citing` reads redirected links), `controller/admission.py` (`check_link`, `check_mapping`, `declaration_refusal` and `derive_failure` read capsules), `controller/requests.py` and `controller/review.py` (`ModelRequest.capsules`), `contracts/operations.py` (the optional field, omitted when empty) and its schema snapshot, `controller/extract.py` and `retrieval/search.py` (redirected-link readers, the `capsule_only` rendering, quoted spans), `privacy/forget.py` (capsule selectors, closure, tombstone targets, surviving roots), `storage/backup.py` (`holds` counts capsules), `service.py` (`show cap_…`, the capsule of a capsule-only event), `operations/health.py` (full-profile `capsules`), `fast/failpoints.py` (`CAPSULE`), `tests/integration/test_retention.py` (`test_expiry_preserves_required_capsule`), `tests/integration/retention_scenarios.py` (capsule owners, the full-profile crash variant), `tests/integration/test_worker.py` (`test_full_profile_capsule_crash_matches_reference`), `tests/integration/worker_scenarios.py`, `tests/coverage_manifest.json`, SYSTEM_SPEC §11, DATA_CONTRACTS §6/§7/§10, IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN Task 15, OPERATIONS §3.
- **Design (decision 0025).** See the decision record. In short: only a `release_profile: full` store compacts; the sweep, after its expiry pass, turns a pinned payload past the 30-day window into a capsule when every live reference can be kept exactly (located quotes by original locator and digest, whole user/operator statements, typed tool-result mappings, identity-only roots); capsule, redirection of the covered claim links and payload removal commit in one transaction with the pins recomputed there (a purge entry in the chain); anything else keeps its pin with a reported reason (`owed_work`, `open_working_context`, `not_a_root`, `source_conflict`, `locator_unresolved`, `mapping_unpreserved`, `unlocated_reference`, `verification_failed`). Capsule IDs derive from the event ID, so no schema migration. The resolver dispatches by `RecordRef.kind` and never slices at old offsets; a capsule is never a root. Released capsules expire with their links reverted; forgetting purges capsules and reaches backups and restore.
- **Two decisions made during implementation:** (1) a pending job whose input is a claim (a review or revalidation) no longer blocks compaction, because it reads what the claim cites and its request now carries the capsule. Under B1 the planner's due reviews never run, and without this no capsule could ever form in a worker run; task/episode/event inputs, unreduced events, rechecks and undebounced failures still block, and expiry still treats every owed event as owed. (2) `ModelRequest.capsules` (optional, omitted when empty): a later review or revalidation of a compacted claim would otherwise have shown the curator no evidence at all.
- **Contract changes.** No schema migration: `SCHEMA_VERSION` stays 11, so existing preview stores and backups stay writable and restorable (not `migration_required`); the reserved `evidence_capsules` table is used as migration 0001 defined it. New contract text: the capsule rules, `preserved_observation` and the table's use (DATA_CONTRACTS §6/§10), `ModelRequest.capsules` (§7; the JSON schema snapshot regenerated, the hook wire unchanged), `show cap_…`. Preview behavior is unchanged: no capsule, 180-day pins, `capsules` unavailable.
- **Tests first.** The new tests were written before the implementation and failed at collection (`cannot import name 'capsules' from 'recalith.privacy'`); the request test failed with `'ModelRequest' object has no attribute 'capsules'` before the field existed. Test-side corrections while implementing, none weakening a check: the day-30 boundary is counted from the oldest capture (as the preview tests do); deferral is asserted per event and reason; the synthetic locator damage covers both of the claim's revisions; health is evaluated at the sweep's time; the request test cancels its first job (unfinished work rightly pins its inputs). The first crash-family run found no compaction in the worker run (every candidate `owed_work`), which led to decision (1) above.
- **Commands and results** (Linux x86_64 container, SQLite 3.45.1, 4 cores; development in a separate git worktree with its own virtualenv and TMPDIR, fast-forwarded into this tree after the baseline):
  - Baseline before any change (a353d83, 3.13): `.venv/bin/python -m pytest -q -rs -p no:cacheprovider` → exit 0, **1220 passed, 23 skipped in 2037 s**.
  - Full suites on 870af5c (3.12 and 3.14 as `TMPDIR=<scratch> UV_PROJECT_ENVIRONMENT=<scratch> uv run --locked --python 3.1x python -m pytest -q -rs -p no:cacheprovider`, all three in parallel, each with its own TMPDIR): 3.13 → exit 1, **1 failed, 1241 passed, 23 skipped in 2275 s**; 3.12 → exit 1, 1 failed, 1241 passed, 23 skipped in 2284 s; 3.14 → exit 1, 1 failed, 1241 passed, 23 skipped in 2198 s. The one failure everywhere was the manifest gate `test_evaluation.py::test_acceptance_manifest_covers_A01_through_A73_by_profile` (`A55/full: an excluded case must be pending or unavailable`): I had moved the preview-excluded A55 to `in_progress`. The gate is right while the full profile is unreachable, so A55 stays `pending` with its tests listed (599e11d); `test_evaluation.py` and `tests/unit/test_coverage_manifest.py` then passed on 3.12, 3.13 and 3.14 (8 passed each).
  - Full suites on the final code (599e11d), all three in parallel: 3.13 → exit 0, **1242 passed, 23 skipped in 2289 s**; 3.12 → exit 0, **1242 passed, 23 skipped in 2302 s**; 3.14 → exit 0, **1242 passed, 23 skipped in 2212 s** (22 more passing than the baseline: the 12 capsule tests, `test_expiry_preserves_required_capsule` and the 9 cases of the new kill/restart matrix). In this run my command left the 3.12 and 3.14 TMPDIR variables pointing at nonexistent directories, so those two used separate pytest base directories under the shared `/tmp` and fresh environments at `/venv-31x`; the logs, environments and the `/tmp/pytest-of-root` tree were moved or removed by path afterwards.
  - Kill/restart at `RECALITH_CRASH_REPS=20` on 599e11d, each with its own TMPDIR: the new full-profile capsule family (`-k capsule_crash`: `failpoints.CAPSULE`, `RETENTION`, `CHAIN`; kills only where retention, capsule or backup work is owed judged from effects, including a payload the reference compacted still available) → exit 0, **9 passed in 802 s**; retention/backup (`-k retention_backup_crash`) → exit 0, **9 passed in 502 s**; forget/restore (`-k forget_restore_crash`) → exit 0, **12 passed in 509 s**; the full-profile chain family (`-k chain_crash`) → exit 0, **9 passed in 329 s**; the worker-boundary family (`-k worker_crash_at_job_boundaries`) → exit 0, **7 passed in 223 s**. At the default 3 repetitions the capsule family took 175 s during development.
