### 2026-10-23 — Task 17 full: the preview-to-full profile transition (decision 0026; Cut 2; commits 1aa30e1, 3f41310, 9cfeae5)

- **Why this task.** B1 is still open: every route in `docs/host-compatibility.json` is `certified: false` (live `not_run` or absent), there is no live `.recalith/host.json`, and the owner's instruction carried no note clearing it. So the live preview gate cannot run, and per instruction Cut 2 continues with Task 17's preview-to-full transition (SYSTEM_SPEC §10.1), the slice that makes a full store reachable for Task 5's chain (decision 0024) and Task 15's capsules (decision 0025). Not started, by instruction: the live gate, the subscription-auth question, Task 14, transfers (export, import, the Breadcrumbs importer), session mode/IPC wake, per-project OS startup entries and every other Cut 2 slice. The starting point (289b0b5) contains decision 0025 (`privacy/capsules.py`, `tests/integration/test_capsules.py`, the checkpoint recording CI run 124); the tree was clean; `.venv` was recreated with `uv sync --locked --python 3.13`. The next free decision number was 0026.
- **Slice boundary and the owner question.** Built: the in-place profile transition by the engine the installation already pins (the supplied wheel must be that release, digest-verified), its journal and recovery, the worker's recovery at start, health reporting. Not built (Task 17 full remainder): the side-by-side engine switch between two releases (second runtime, `engine.lock`/launcher switch, the bootstrap's upgrade entry point), same-profile upgrades, schema migration of older stores, per-project OS startup entries. **Owner question (not guessed):** whether to make the full profile available to real installations. Recommendation: not yet — keep `FULL_RELEASE_AVAILABLE = False` (one build constant gating both `init --release-profile full` and `recalith upgrade`), because the rest of the full release is not built and without IPC wake the full profile's 30-second idle poll would miss the typed-state target. Flipping it is one line; nothing else depends on it.
- **Files.**
  - New: `src/recalith/operations/upgrade.py`, `tests/integration/upgrade_scenarios.py`, `docs/decisions/0026-preview-to-full-transition.md`.
  - Changed: `core/identity.py` (`load_project_config(…, release_profile=)` with the `[retention]` overrides, `store_profile`, `installation_config`, `record_profile`), `privacy/retention.py` and `controller/worker.py` (configuration from `installation_config`; the worker's `recover_maintenance` before it opens the store), `operations/health.py` (configuration from the store's profile; `recovery.coverage.upgrade`, `upgrade_recovery_pending`, the marker `active` while a transition is journaled), `privacy/forget.py` (`profile_transition` content-free; the marker kept while a transition is journaled; restore refused while one is; the worker-stop message names its purpose), `storage/integrity.py` (`content_digest`, `verify_view`, `record_checked_head`), `fast/failpoints.py` (`UPGRADE` family; constants only), `service.py` (`upgrade`; the init gate reads `FULL_RELEASE_AVAILABLE`), `cli/mutate.py` (the `upgrade` form dispatches to the service), `tests/integration/test_upgrade.py` (the six pending skips replaced; 28 tests), `tests/integration/test_worker.py` (`test_profile_transition_crash_matches_reference`), `tests/integration/worker_scenarios.py` (the upgrade scenario), `tests/coverage_manifest.json` (A02, A40, A21, A35), SYSTEM_SPEC §2.2/§10.1, DATA_CONTRACTS §11/§12, IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN Task 17, OPERATIONS §3.
- **Design (decision 0026).** In short: refusals before any effect (downgrade `policy_refused`; same profile, another schema or another engine `capability_unavailable`; wheel name/digest `invalid_schema`); exclusive maintenance (privacy lock, worker stopped, unfinished forget finished, marker, journal `.recalith/run/upgrade.json`); a verified backup with the current ledger; capability registration (eligible `revision_chain`/`capsules`; Codex, generic JSON and MCP not activated; the provider route probed with version observation; delivery exactly as certified); staging in memory from a consistent snapshot: exact capsules or retained pins under the target window, then one activation transaction (profile, policy revision and guidance epoch, the content-free `profile_transition` autonomy log entry, the chain's activation baseline last), evidence availability and a full staged chain check; the staged file beside the live store; the journal's `activating` record is the commit point, then a content-digest fence and one atomic rename; completion writes the verifier checkpoint of the staged check, the tracked `release_profile` record, the ledger copy and releases the marker. Before the commit point any failure or crash rolls back (`rolled_back`; `not_activated` without a backup); from it recovery activates and completes; only a complete preview or a complete full store ever serves. The store's profile governs an installation's defaults; explicit retention overrides are a `project.toml` `[retention]` table and survive the transition.
- **Contract changes.** No schema migration: `SCHEMA_VERSION` stays 11; preview stores and backups stay writable and restorable; a `migration_required` store is refused by the transition unchanged (the general schema upgrade is not built). No contract model changed: `UpgradeResult` keeps its fields (`retained_pin_count` = pinned payloads past the target window still held in full; per-reason deferrals are in the autonomy log entry), the JSON schema snapshots and the hook wire are unchanged. New contract text: the `[retention]` table and the store-authoritative profile (`ProjectConfig`), the transition's states and error codes, `recovery.coverage.upgrade`, the CLI's `upgrade` exits (4 until the full release; 6 for a downgrade request on a full store).
- **Tests first.** The new tests were written before the implementation and failed at collection: `ImportError: cannot import name 'upgrade' from 'recalith.operations'` (test_upgrade.py) and `AttributeError: module 'recalith.fast.failpoints' has no attribute 'UPGRADE'` (test_worker.py). Test-side corrections while implementing, none weakening a check: per-reason deferral counts are lower bounds (other pinned events share the fixture's reasons); `operations.health.health` (not the service wrapper) takes `now`; the rollback test runs past the 30-day window so the staged compaction transaction (and its failpoint) runs; worker readiness is `wait_running` (a running worker reports `running` or `idle`); the jobs test uses `extract_claims` jobs (a revalidation of a nonexistent claim is settled without a model, as before) and a preparer that reads the current read set — the fixture's fixed policy-1 read set is exactly what the transition fences; the kill at `upgrade.before_swap` recovers to `activated` (the commit point), not `rolled_back`. Two implementation fixes from the tests: the capability registry ignores the transition's own maintenance marker; a failure after the rename inside the swap completes instead of rolling back. A deliberate mutation (recording the tracked profile before the swap) failed the new crash family at `upgrade.before_swap` with `project_toml_ahead_of_store`, then was reverted.
- **Commands and results** (Linux x86_64 container, SQLite 3.45.1, 4 cores; development in a separate git worktree with its own virtualenv and TMPDIR, fast-forwarded into this tree; the container restarted once during the first full runs, which were re-run whole):
  - Baseline before any change (289b0b5, 3.13): `.venv/bin/python -m pytest -q -rs -p no:cacheprovider` → exit 0, **1242 passed, 23 skipped in 2368 s**.
  - Full suites on 3f41310, all three in parallel, each with its own TMPDIR (and `UV_PROJECT_ENVIRONMENT` for 3.12/3.14, every variable set inside its own backgrounded command): 3.13 → exit 0, **1281 passed, 17 skipped in 3522 s**; `uv run --locked --python 3.12 python -m pytest -q -rs -p no:cacheprovider` → exit 0, **1281 passed, 17 skipped in 3560 s**; the same with `--python 3.14` → exit 0, **1281 passed, 17 skipped in 3441 s** (39 more passing and 6 fewer skipped than the baseline: the six pending upgrade placeholders became 28 tests in `test_upgrade.py` and the 14 cases of the new kill/restart matrix; the suites ran about 50 % longer than last session's on this restarted container, with the same three in parallel).
  - Kill/restart at `RECALITH_CRASH_REPS=20` on 3f41310, each with its own TMPDIR (four in parallel, then two): the new transition family (`-k profile_transition_crash`: `failpoints.UPGRADE`, the backup family's four points, `CHAIN`; kills only where transition or post-transition work is owed, judged from effects) → exit 0, **14 passed in 1483 s**; capsule (`-k capsule_crash`) → exit 0, **9 passed in 1226 s**; retention/backup (`-k retention_backup_crash`) → exit 0, **9 passed in 752 s**; forget/restore (`-k forget_restore_crash`) → exit 0, **12 passed in 755 s**; chain (`-k chain_crash`) → exit 0, **9 passed in 487 s**; worker boundaries (`-k worker_crash_at_job_boundaries`) → exit 0, **7 passed in 328 s**. At the default 3 repetitions the transition family took 239 s during development.
  - `uvx ruff@0.14.0 check --select F,E9,B` on every touched Python file → clean. `tools/generate_schemas.py --check` → exit 0; `tools/generate_hook_wire.py --check` → exit 0 (no contract model changed). `tools/release_checklist.py` → exit 0, verdict `blocked`, `problems: []`; `inference_isolation_and_delivery_certified`, `smoke_quality_gates`, `deterministic_case_variants` and `release_artifacts` still `blocked` (B1 and owner decisions), unchanged.
  - §7 picture: `fast/failpoints.py` (constants) and the worker's start changed, so both tools were re-run here on a quiet machine after the crash families (output to scratch first). The first attempt passed a directory to `--output` and failed writing its report (exit 1 after 192 s and 1251 s; nothing measured was kept), so both were re-run whole: `tools/measure_release_latency.py --output <scratch>/release.json` → exit 0 in 213 s, gate met (warm and cold-missing), warm entry-to-exit p95 42.8 ms (wall 133.3 ms), cold-missing 46.7 ms, concurrent hooks 41.3 ms, busy writer 37.6 ms; `tools/measure_worker_latency.py --idle-seconds 600 --output <scratch>/worker.json` → exit 0 in 1253 s, typed-state p95 **4.80 s** from the hook spawn (n=100; 4.79 s on 599e11d), idle 0.21 % of one core, 47.4 MiB. The hook latencies are higher than last session's 27.3 ms on this container, which restarted mid-session and ran the suites about 50 % slower; an A/B of the same tool on this host, back to back, attributes it to the host: baseline 289b0b5 warm p95 44.6 ms (wall 136.9 ms), this slice 48.4 ms (wall 137.0 ms), both gates met in both. The tool left its `recalith-worker-latency-*` directory under the scratch TMPDIR; it was removed by path after checking no process used it.
- **CI:** run 128 (3f41310): Linux 3.12 **1282 passed, 16 skipped in 1802 s**; Linux 3.13 **1282 passed, 16 skipped in 1475 s** (and the offline provisioning step: 1 passed); Linux 3.14 **1282 passed, 16 skipped in 2119 s**; macOS 3.13 **1282 passed, 16 skipped in 1961 s**; Windows 3.13 **1 failed, 1215 passed, 82 skipped in 6239 s**: `test_upgrade_A_leaves_B_running` read B's `run/worker.lock`, which B's running worker holds with a byte-range lock on Windows (`PermissionError`), before excluding `run/` from the comparison — a test defect (POSIX locks are advisory, so Linux and macOS read it); fixed in 9cfeae5 by skipping the worker's directories before opening anything (B's store is compared through its semantic state, its worker by PID). The CI run of the fix is recorded in the follow-up docs commit. `release-artifacts` run 23 green; `measure` run 30 green on all three runners: typed-state p95 from the hook spawn Linux 4.51 s, Windows 4.54 s, macOS 4.68 s (n=20 each); idle CPU 0.063 / 0.21 / 0.22 % of one core, RSS 47.1 / 16.3 / 41.8 MiB; hook gates met on Windows and macOS (warm entry-to-exit p95 64.9 / 24.3 ms); **on Linux the warm gate was not met** (6 of 500 warm hooks degraded, 1.2 % against ≤ 1 %, delivered 98.8 % against ≥ 99 %; reasons `deadline_delivery_skipped` 4, `deadline_lookup_skipped` 3, `deadline_dependency_unknown` 2, `capture_deadline_overrun` 1, `scratch_lock_timeout` 1) from a burst of nine consecutive samples (148–156) at 236–416 ms against about 11 ms otherwise, plus two isolated ones — a runner stall: the hook path's code did not change (only failpoint constants were added), the local runs meet the gate, and the next measure run is recorded in the follow-up docs commit.
- **Acceptance** (none passed; the transition is not available to real installations and the engine switch is not built): **A02** full_only `pending` → `in_progress`; **A40** full_only `pending` → `in_progress`; **A21** gains the transition's kill/restart family; **A35** full_only gains `test_failed_capsule_transition_keeps_pin`; **A55** stays `pending` (preview-excluded while the full profile is unreachable). `tests/coverage_manifest.json` lists the tests.
- **Gaps and limits:**
  - **B1** is unchanged; health still reports inference unavailable.
  - **Full profile not available** to real installations (owner question above); `init --release-profile full` and every `upgrade` form except a downgrade request answer 4.
  - **Not built:** the side-by-side engine switch, same-profile upgrades, schema migration of older stores, per-project OS startup entries.
  - **Fence window:** the content-digest check and the rename are separate steps (as in restore); the worker is stopped and hooks never write SQLite.
  - **Installed host:** Claude Code 2.1.308 remains uncertified; its probes were not re-run (nothing on the provider launch or delivery path changed).
- **Next action:** the operator's live certification (B1 commands), then the live preview gate; without B1, the owner's answer on enabling the full profile, or the next Cut 2 slice the owner selects.

## Blockers and decisions

**B1 — Task 0/0b live certification (open).** Evidence: `docs/host-compatibility.json` (`auth_route.kind = not_configured`, live `not_run`). This cloud session has the Claude CLI but no explicitly configured build account route; the session's own host credentials/proxy are not a Recalith route and are not used. Minimal operator action, from an ordinary terminal outside any agent session:

1. Copy `tools/probe_config.example.json` to `probe-private/local-config.json`; set `executable`, the explicit `model` for both sections, the auth route key names (API key route with `--bare`; see decision 0002), and `operator_attestation` (`checked_eligible` with `basis`/`checked_on` only after checking the account's terms for unattended background use).
2. `export ANTHROPIC_API_KEY=...` (or the selected key name) in that terminal.
3. `python tools/probe_provider.py --config probe-private/local-config.json --output docs/host-compatibility.json --route-out /path/to/project` (expect exit 0 when certified; `--route-out` records the project's `.recalith/provider.json`, certified only after this complete live pass, decision 0009).
4. `python tools/check_provider_runtime.py --config probe-private/local-config.json --output docs/host-compatibility.json` (the production adapter's own checks and the route status; exit 0 only for a certified route).
5. `python tools/probe_delivery.py --config probe-private/local-config.json --output docs/host-compatibility.json` (exit 0 requires healthy start/prompt).

**Operator commands to complete live certification** (an ordinary OS terminal outside any agent or IDE session; no marker such as `CLAUDECODE` in its environment):

1. Choose the curator backend and copy its example to `probe-private/local-config.json` (`docs/OPERATIONS.md` §4, `docs/probes/LOCAL_CONFIG.md`):
   - Claude CLI: `tools/probe_config.example.json` — `executable`, `expected_version` set to the installed `claude --version`, the certified argument array;
   - Anthropic API key: `tools/probe_config.anthropic_messages.example.json` (`backend: anthropic_messages`);
   - OpenAI-compatible provider: `tools/probe_config.openai_compatible.example.json` — the provider's full `…/chat/completions` URL, its auth header, `expected_resolved_model` if it reports a different identifier;
   - local server: `tools/probe_config.local_server.example.json` — a loopback `http` URL, `auth_route: none`, attestation `not_applicable_local`.
   In every case set the explicit `model` (a placeholder in the examples; no model name is built in), an `auth_route` naming only the key's environment variable, and `operator_attestation` `checked_eligible` with `basis`/`checked_on` only after checking the provider's terms for unattended background use. Set the `delivery` section for the working agent's Claude Code host.
2. `export THE_SELECTED_KEY_NAME=...` in that terminal only (none for a keyless local server). For a hard spending stop, also turn off auto-reload or overage billing in the provider's console.
3. `python tools/probe_provider.py --config probe-private/local-config.json --backend BACKEND --output docs/host-compatibility.json --route-out /path/to/project`. Expect exit 0; this writes the project's certified `.recalith/provider.json`.
4. `python tools/check_provider_runtime.py --config probe-private/local-config.json --backend BACKEND --output docs/host-compatibility.json`. Expect exit 0.
5. In the project: `.recalith/bin/recalith integrate claude`. This is local and needs the terminal, or `--operator-token-file PATH`.
6. `python tools/probe_delivery.py --config probe-private/local-config.json --engine-project /path/to/project --output docs/host-compatibility.json`. Exit 0 requires live start/prompt healthy. It writes the project's `live` `.recalith/host.json` and re-runs the integration so exactly the certified paths are enabled.
7. `.recalith/bin/recalith status`. It exits 0 only when every required capability is healthy.

Proceeding independently: Task 1 contracts and the deterministic kernel (Tasks 2–6, 8) do not depend on the live route. Autonomous extraction (Task 9 onward with a real provider) and any readiness claim stay blocked until B1 clears.

For each further blocker, record the task, observed evidence, minimal missing action or access, and what can proceed independently. Keep secrets and raw private data out of this file. Place lasting policy/interface decisions in `docs/decisions/` and update the owning normative document.

## Next-session handoff

Task 18's preview slice, the Task 11 follow-up (decision 0021), the Windows typed-state slice (decision 0022), the direct-API inference backends (decision 0023) and, in Cut 2, Task 5's full slice (decision 0024: the revision chain and integrity scanner) Task 15's capsule slice (decision 0025: exact evidence capsules) and Task 17's preview-to-full transition (decision 0026), all full profile only, are built; see their checkpoints. The transition is reached only through `operations.upgrade.transition` (the tests); the CLI and `init --release-profile full` answer 4 until the owner enables the full profile.

- The preview gate is **blocked** by B1.
- Every healthy §7 target is met on every native platform on b3f1f1f: hook latency and usefulness, typed-state p95 ≤ 5 s and the idle footprint. The checklist item `hook_latency_usefulness_per_platform` is `passed`.

Next actions, in order:

1. **The operator's live certification of their chosen curator route, then the live preview gate.** Run the eight operator commands under B1 from an ordinary terminal (any backend: Claude CLI, Anthropic Messages, OpenAI-compatible or a local server), then record the live results here.
   - The installed host is now Claude Code 2.1.308, which needs a fixture capture and review before it is certified (decision 0019).
   - Re-run `integrate claude` after any host upgrade.
2. **Watch two Windows tests.**
   - `test_concurrent_hooks_preserve_budget` failed once on a slow Windows runner (CI run 107) with a reason the assertion did not name. It now names it; attribute it on the next occurrence.
   - A Windows typed-state p95 over 5 s on a later run is read from `poll.timing` and the observer's stall records first (decision 0022).
3. **Owner decisions before publication:** the project license and the signing material (the release artifacts are unsigned).
4. **Owner question: enable the full profile for real installations?** Recommendation: not yet. Keep `FULL_RELEASE_AVAILABLE = False` in `operations/upgrade.py` (it gates both `init --release-profile full` and `recalith upgrade`) until the rest of the full release exists; without IPC wake the full profile's 30-second idle poll would miss the typed-state target. Flipping it is one line plus the CLI tests' expectations.
5. **Cut 2 continues** with the slice the owner selects next. Built: Task 5's full slice, Task 15's capsule slice and Task 17's transition. Natural candidates: the side-by-side engine switch (Task 17's remainder) or IPC wake and session mode (Task 4 full), which the full profile's defaults assume.
6. **Watch the Linux warm hook gate.** Measure run 30 missed it on Linux from one runner stall (decision 0026 checkpoint); read the next runs' degradation reasons and sample series before attributing anything to code.

**Moving to local work (Windows laptop, WSL2 likely).**

- **Where to run the suite and the crash families.** Prefer WSL2 (Ubuntu) with the clone in the Linux filesystem (for example `~/src/Recalith`), never under `/mnt/c`: the Windows drive is a 9p (drvfs) mount, which `identity.require_local_filesystem` refuses for a live store (`storage_failure`), and SQLite's WAL locking and fsync are not reliable there. In WSL2 the commands are the Linux ones in the checkpoints: `uv sync --locked --python 3.13`, then `.venv/bin/python -m pytest -q -rs -p no:cacheprovider`; the crash families with `RECALITH_CRASH_REPS=20` and `-k <family>`, each run with its own `TMPDIR` and, for 3.12/3.14, its own `UV_PROJECT_ENVIRONMENT`, all set inside the command that runs in the background. Native Windows is what CI's `windows` job runs (about 88 skips, about twice as long); run it locally only to reproduce a Windows-only result, from PowerShell in a clone on an NTFS drive (`uv run --locked --python 3.13 python -m pytest -q -rs -p no:cacheprovider`). The Windows-only mechanisms (Job Object breakaway, the console-handle guard test, the Windows typed-state measurement of decision 0022) are exercised only natively.
- **Where the B1 operator commands run.** In an ordinary terminal of the same OS environment as the Claude Code host the working agent uses: a WSL2 shell if Claude Code runs inside WSL2, a native PowerShell or Windows Terminal if it runs natively; never in a terminal an agent or IDE session started. Check that the environment carries no host marker (`env | grep -i claude` in WSL2, `Get-ChildItem Env: | Where-Object Name -like '*CLAUDE*'` in PowerShell) before steps 3–8. The project root must be on that environment's local filesystem. Export the key in that terminal only.
- **What stays out of the repository.** `probe-private/` (the local probe config), every real `.recalith/` private file (`provider.json`, `host.json`, `local.toml`, `state/`, `spool/`, `run/`, `backups/`, `exports/`, `runtime/`), operator token files, API keys and `.env` files, raw transcripts (for example under `~/.claude/projects/`), and measurement or probe outputs that hold machine paths or user names until sanitized. Write measurement and probe output to a scratch directory first; commit only sanitized evidence. The root `.gitignore` already excludes `probe-private/`, `.env*`, `/.claude/settings.local.json` and the private `.recalith/` paths; check `git status` before every commit anyway.
